<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let <i>X</i> be a projective ℚ-factorial terminal threefold over an algebraically closed field of characteristic <i>p</i> &gt; 3. We prove the numerical-dimension-one case of abundance: if <i>K</i><sub><i>X</i></sub> is nef with $`\nu(K_X)=1`$, then <i>K</i><sub><i>X</i></sub> is semiample and $`\kappa(X,K_X)=1`$.

- **claim-numerical-dimension-one-abundance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X$ be a normal projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial threefold over an algebraically closed field $k$ of characteristic $p>3$. Suppose that $X$ has terminal singularities and that $K_X$ is nef with $\nu(K_X)=1$. Then some positive Cartier multiple of $K_X$ has at least two independent global sections. In particular, $\kappa(X,K_X)=1$ and $K_X$ is semiample.
  - Explanation: Under the stated geometric and characteristic hypotheses, a pluricanonical multiple has independent sections, the Kodaira dimension equals the numerical dimension, and the canonical divisor is semiample.
- **claim-prepared-primitive-cycle** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-prepared-cycle)): Suppose that $k$ is uncountable and algebraically closed of characteristic $p>3$, and that $X$ is a projective terminal threefold with $K_X$ nef, $\nu(K_X)=1$, and $n(X,K_X)=3$. There exist a smooth projective threefold $V$, a generically finite separable morphism $\rho:V\to X$, a very ample divisor $H$ on $V$, and a nonzero effective Cartier divisor $$D=\sum_{\alpha}m_\alpha D_\alpha,\qquad m_\alpha\in\mathbb Z_{>0},\qquad \gcd_\alpha m_\alpha=1,$$ with the following properties.* 1. *The support of $D$ is connected and has simple normal crossings. The divisor $D$ is nef, and $D\|_{D_\alpha}$ is numerically trivial for every $\alpha$.* 2. *There is no $D$-trivial curve through a very general point of $V$. Moreover $D\cdot H^2>0$ and $D^2\cdot H=0$.* 3. *The invertible sheaf $\mathcal O_D(D)$ is torsion. Its order will be denoted by $r$.* 4. *For an integer $b\geq0$, an open neighborhood $U$ of $\|D\|$, and integers $b_\alpha$, there is an isomorphism $$\label{eq:prepared-canonical} \omega_V^{\otimes p^b}\|_U \simeq \mathcal O_U(B_*),\qquad B_*=\sum_\alpha b_\alpha D_\alpha .$$ Consequently $K_V\cdot D\cdot A=0$ for every Cartier divisor $A$ on $V$.
  - Explanation: The hypothetical maximal nef dimension branch produces smooth prepared data with a connected primitive nef cycle, torsion on the full Cartier cycle, and a local canonical relation.
- **claim-exceptional-intersection-negativity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exceptional-intersections)): Let $P$ be an excellent normal local surface with closed residue field $l$, and let $g:T\to P$ be a nontrivial projective regular resolution which is an isomorphism away from the closed point. Write $G_i$ for its exceptional integral curves, and take intersection degrees over $l$. Then $G_i^2<0$ for every $i$. More generally, if an exceptional real divisor $Z=\sum_i z_iG_i$ has a positive coefficient, there is an index $j$ with $z_j>0$ and $Z\cdot G_j<0$.
  - Explanation: Exceptional curves have negative self-intersection. An exceptional real divisor with a positive coefficient has negative intersection with a component carrying a positive coefficient.
- **claim-terminal-singularities-isolated** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-terminal-codimension-two)): A terminal threefold in the present setting has only isolated singularities.
  - Explanation: Terminality rules out singularities in codimension two, leaving only isolated singularities on the threefold.
- **claim-torsion-on-full-cartier-cycle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-full-cycle-torsion)): Let $Y$ be a normal projective threefold over an algebraically closed field of characteristic $p>0$, and let $L$ be an effective Cartier divisor. Suppose that $L$ is numerically trivial on every integral component of its support and that $\mathcal O_Y(L)\|_{L_{\mathrm{red}}\setminus Z}$ is trivial for a finite set of closed points $Z$. Then $\mathcal O_L(L)$ is torsion.
  - Explanation: Numerical triviality on the components and a trivialization away from finitely many reduced-boundary points imply torsion on the entire nonreduced Cartier divisor.
- **claim-connection-detects-destabilization** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-connection-destabilization)): Let $E$ be a semistable torsion-free sheaf. If $F_V^*E$ is unstable and $B$ is its first Harder–Narasimhan term, then the connection induces a nonzero morphism on a big open set $$B\longrightarrow (F_V^*E/B)\otimes\Omega_V^1.$$
  - Explanation: If a Frobenius pullback becomes unstable, its canonical connection cannot preserve the first destabilizing term: preservation would descend a subsheaf contradicting semistability.
- **claim-destabilizing-term-is-foliation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-destabilizing-foliation)): If $T_V$ is unstable, its first Harder–Narasimhan term $A$ is closed under Lie brackets and under $p$-th powers of derivations.
  - Explanation: The first destabilizing tangent subsheaf is closed under brackets and characteristic powers, so it defines the distribution needed for an inseparable quotient.
- **claim-foliation-quotient-canonical-formula** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-foliation-quotient)): Let $A\subset T_V$ be a saturated bracket-closed and $p$-closed subsheaf of rank $d$. There is a finite purely inseparable map $\pi\colon V\to W$ to a normal projective variety, with a factorization $$V\xrightarrow{\pi}W\xrightarrow{\rho}V^{(1)}$$ of relative Frobenius. On open sets whose complements have codimension at least two, the canonical bundles satisfy $$\omega_V\simeq\pi^*\omega_W\otimes(\det A)^{\otimes(p-1)}. \label{eq:foliation-canonical}$$
  - Explanation: A saturated distribution with the stated closure properties produces a finite purely inseparable quotient factoring Frobenius, together with the canonical bundle identity on a big open set.
- **claim-tangent-strong-semistability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-strong-semistability)): The tangent bundle $T_V$ is strongly semistable for the slope $c_1(-)HD/\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(-)$. Moreover, $$c_2(V)D\ge0. \label{eq:c2-nonnegative}$$
  - Explanation: The prepared cycle prevents destabilizing foliations. The tangent bundle remains semistable under Frobenius pullback, and the Bogomolov inequality gives the stated nonnegative Chern intersection.
- **claim-nonpositive-degree-line-sections** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-degree-zero-line-sections)): If a line bundle $L$ has $\deg L\le0$, then $h^0(V,L)\le1$.
  - Explanation: Independent sections would produce a pencil whose components contain curves of trivial cycle degree through a very general point, contradicting the prepared geometry.
- **claim-semistable-hom-rank-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-semistable-hom-bound)): If $G_1,G_2$ are torsion-free semistable sheaves of slope zero, then $$\dim_k\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(G_1,G_2)\le\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(G_1)\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(G_2). \label{eq:hom-rank-bound}$$
  - Explanation: The section bound for determinant lines controls morphisms between stable factors; filtrations then bound the Hom dimension by the product of the ranks.
- **claim-uniform-polar-part-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-polar-bounds)): Let $G$ be a semistable vector bundle of slope zero. For every integer $i$, $$h^0\bigl(V,G(iD)\bigr)\le\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}G, \qquad h^0\bigl(V,G^{\otimes2}(iD)\bigr)\le(\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}G)^2. \label{eq:uniform-bundle-sections}$$ For each fixed bundle $J=G$ or $J=G^{\otimes2}$, the increasing spaces $$H^0\bigl(V,J(iD)/J\bigr),\qquad i=1,2,\ldots, \label{eq:polar-part-system}$$ have bounded dimension and eventually stabilize under their natural injections. In particular their elements have a uniform bound on their actual pole orders along every prime component of $D$. This pole bound may depend on $J$; it is uniform in the cut $i$ and in the chosen section.
  - Explanation: Section dimensions are bounded by ranks, and the increasing spaces of polar parts stabilize. Their actual pole orders are therefore uniformly bounded for each fixed bundle.
- **claim-scalar-polar-parts-vanish** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-no-scalar-poles)): For every $i>0$, $$H^0\bigl(V,\mathcal O_V(iD)/\mathcal O_V\bigr)=0.$$
  - Explanation: A genuine scalar pole would acquire unbounded order under characteristic powers, contradicting the uniform polar-part bound.
- **claim-finite-page-calculus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-page-calculus)): The operation $x\mapsto\partial x$ induces maps $$d_s:E_s^{i,a}(E)\longrightarrow E_s^{i-s,a+1}(E)$$ whose cohomology is the next page. At page one, $E_1^{i,a}(E)=H^a(V,P_i^1(E))$; every page has only rows $a=0,1,2$. In particular, with $\delta_s^a(i)=g_s^a(i;E)-g_{s+1}^a(i;E)$ for $a=0,2$, $$\begin{aligned} g_s^0(i;E) &=\dim\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl(H^0(P_i^s(E))\longrightarrow H^0(P_i^1(E))\bigr), \label{eq:bottom-endpoint}\\ g_s^2(i;E)&=g_s^0(1-i;E^!),\label{eq:top-endpoint}\\ g_{s+1}^1(i;E) &=g_s^1(i;E)-\delta_s^0(i+s)-\delta_s^2(i-s). \label{eq:middle-recurrence} \end{aligned}$$ If $M>0$ and $\mathcal O(MD)\|_{sD}$ is trivial, the page-$s$ ranks are periodic in $i$ modulo $M$, and $$\label{eq:periodic-euler} \sum_{i\bmod M}\bigl(g_s^0(i;E)-g_s^1(i;E)+g_s^2(i;E)\bigr) =\sum_{i\bmod M}\chi(P_i^1(E)).$$ Finally, at each fixed weight $i$, the stable image in Equation <a href="#eq:bottom-endpoint" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:bottom-endpoint">[eq:bottom-endpoint]</a> is precisely the image of the global sections of the completed bundle $\widehat{E(iD)}$ in $H^0(P_i^1(E))$.
  - Explanation: The pole filtration supplies page differentials, endpoint duality, the middle-row recurrence, periodic Euler sums, and a stabilized-image description of completed sections.
- **claim-scalar-jet-uniform-pole-rule** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-jets)): Let $u<i$ be integers and let $\xi\in H^0(\mathcal O(iD)/\mathcal O(uD))$ be nonzero. There is an integer $t$ with $u<t\leq i$ such that local lifts of $\xi$ have pole order exactly $tm_\alpha$ at every $D_\alpha$. Those lifts give a trivializing section of $\mathcal O(tD)$ on $(t-u)D$. In particular, $$\label{eq:scalar-endpoint} g_s^0(i;\mathcal O_V)= \begin{cases} 1,&\mathcal O(iD)\|_{sD}\simeq\mathcal O_{sD},\\ 0,&\text{otherwise}. \end{cases}$$
  - Explanation: A nonzero scalar jet has a common integral pole ratio on all components and trivializes the corresponding line on a Cartier thickening. This determines the scalar leading-image ranks.
- **claim-arbitrarily-wide-canonical-jets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-forced-canonical-jets)): There are nonzero leading jets of $\omega_V$ of arbitrarily large width, allowing their upper weight to vary.
  - Explanation: Eventual disappearance of all canonical leading jets would make consecutive scalar-drop patterns incompatible with the middle-row recurrence.
- **claim-integral-canonical-formal-root** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-canonical-formal-root)): Every coefficient of $B_*$ in Equation <a href="#eq:canonical-p-power" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:canonical-p-power">[eq:canonical-p-power]</a> is divisible by $p^b$. Writing $B_*=p^bB_0$ with $B_0$ integral, there is an isomorphism on the completion along $D$, $$\label{eq:canonical-formal-root} \widehat{\omega}_V\simeq\widehat{\mathcal O}_V(B_0).$$
  - Explanation: Wide canonical jets force divisibility of the supported canonical coefficients. Compatible trivializations on finite thickenings then identify the completed canonical line with an integral divisor line.
- **claim-limiting-width-and-chern-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-scalar-widths)): The scalar widths satisfy $$\label{eq:limiting-width} a_*=\lim_{e\to\infty}\frac{d_e}{M_e} =\frac{p}{p+1},\qquad Dc_2(V)=0.$$
  - Explanation: Counting endpoint drops with multiplicity forces the Chern intersection to vanish and fixes the limiting ratio of last trivialization widths.
- **claim-leading-jet-phase-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-phase-bound)): Let $G$ be a vector bundle that is semistable of slope zero for the degree $c_1(-)\cdot H\cdot D$. There is a constant $A_G$ such that, for every sufficiently large $e$, $$g^0_{\lfloor c_0M_e\rfloor}(i;G)>0 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(i,M_e\mathbb Z)\le A_G.$$
  - Explanation: Long leading jets of a semistable bundle occur only at uniformly bounded distance from the period lattice. Otherwise a translated jet or its tensor power would violate the polar bound.
- **claim-laurent-cohomology-vanishing** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-laurent-vanishing)): For the prepared data and periods fixed above, let $E$ be a vector bundle on $V$ with the following properties:* 1. *$\chi(P_i^1(E))=0$ for every integer $i$.* 2. *For $J=E,E^!$, the spaces $H^0(V,J(nD)/J)$ have dimensions bounded independently of the positive integer $n$.* 3. *For $J=E,E^!$ there is a constant $A_J$ such that $$g^0_{u_e}(i;J)>0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(i,M_e\mathbb Z)\le A_J$$ for all sufficiently large $e$.* *Then global sections of $E_{\mathcal R}$ form a finite-dimensional $k$-vector space. Every Laurent Čech $1$-cocycle on a finite affine cover adapted to the bundles and the divisor has an actual Laurent primitive. The same assertion holds after refining such a cover.
  - Explanation: Euler vanishing, bounded polar parts for the bundle and its dualizing twist, and concentration of leading jets imply finite-dimensional Laurent sections and actual bounded-pole primitives.
- **claim-cartier-algebra-on-laurent-charts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-laurent-cartier-algebra)): On a smooth affine chart with étale coordinates $x_1,x_2,x_3$ and a local equation $f$ for $D$, put $R=\widehat A[1/f]$. The monomials $x^I=x_1^{I_1}x_2^{I_2}x_3^{I_3}$, $0\le I_j<p$, form a basis of $R$ over $R^p$. Ordinary differentials and the Cartier isomorphism extend to this ring. The algebraic bundles $B^1,Z^1$ and their defining exact sequences extend to the corresponding Laurent exact and closed forms, with their Frobenius module structure. In particular, $$\label{eq:cartier-sequences} 0\longrightarrow\mathcal O_V\longrightarrow F_*\mathcal O_V \longrightarrow B^1\longrightarrow0, \qquad 0\longrightarrow B^1\longrightarrow Z^1 \xrightarrow{C}\Omega\longrightarrow0$$ are sequences of vector bundles and remain exact on Laurent charts. The images and kernels in the Frobenius de Rham complex are locally free, as are the relevant quotients. Moreover, $$\label{eq:frobenius-duality} (F_*\mathcal O_V)^\vee\otimes\omega_V\simeq F_*\omega_V.$$
  - Explanation: The coordinate basis over characteristic powers survives completion and localization. Cartier theory, local freeness, exact sequences, and Frobenius duality therefore remain valid on Laurent charts.
- **claim-laurent-form-cocycles-have-primitives** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-form-vanishing)): Every Laurent Čech $1$-cocycle with values in $\Omega$, $B^1$, or $Z^1$ has a Laurent primitive. Global Laurent one-forms form a finite-dimensional $k$-vector space.
  - Explanation: The Laurent criterion applies to ordinary and exact forms, and the Cartier sequence supplies the assertion for closed forms. Global Laurent one-forms are finite-dimensional.
- **claim-global-cartier-adjustment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cartier-adjustment)): Every global Laurent one-form is closed. On the finite-dimensional $k$-vector space $$W=H^0(\|D\|,\Omega^1_{\mathcal R}),$$ Cartier is a bijective inverse-Frobenius-semilinear map, and $C-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}:W\to W$ is surjective as an additive map. Moreover, every additive $\mathcal Z^1$-valued Čech one-cocycle is a coboundary after refining its open cover.
  - Explanation: Laurent cocycle vanishing and finite-dimensionality make global forms closed and Cartier bijective. Algebraic closedness gives surjectivity of its additive difference from the identity.
- **claim-cartier-fixed-line-connections** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-cartier-fixed-connection)): Every $\mathcal R$-line bundle admits an integrable connection whose local connection one-forms are fixed by Cartier.
  - Explanation: Logarithmic transition differentials are solved as a closed-form cocycle, then a global Cartier adjustment makes the resulting integrable connection Cartier-fixed.
- **claim-cartier-fixed-curvature-vanishes** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-cartier-curvature)): If $\alpha=\sum_i a_i\,dx_i$ is closed and $C\alpha=\alpha$, then the connection operators $$D_i=\partial_i+a_i:R\longrightarrow R$$ commute and satisfy $$D_i^p=0, \qquad [D_i,x_j]=\delta_{ij}. \label{eq:horizontal-relations}$$ Here a ring element in an operator formula denotes multiplication by that element.
  - Explanation: For a closed Cartier-fixed form, the rank-one operator identity annihilates characteristic powers of the connection operators; closedness supplies their commutativity.
- **claim-horizontal-expansion-and-descent** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-horizontal-expansion)): Let $M$ be a free rank-one $R$-module with a connection whose operators satisfy Equation <a href="#eq:horizontal-relations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:horizontal-relations">[eq:horizontal-relations]</a> and commute. Its horizontal module $$H_M=\bigcap_i\ker(D_i:M\to M)$$ is a finite projective rank-one $S$-module, and multiplication gives an isomorphism $$R\otimes_S H_M\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }M. \label{eq:horizontal-descent}$$ Formation of $H_M$ commutes with restriction to a smaller Laurent chart retaining the coordinates.
  - Explanation: A horizontal projector and truncated Taylor expansion identify the line module with scalar extension of its horizontal module. The construction commutes with restriction.
- **claim-formal-line-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-formal-line-extension)): For the smooth affine chart and rings $A,B,R$ above, $B$ is a regular Noetherian ring. Every finite projective rank-one $R$-module extends to a finite projective rank-one $B$-module. Its associated line is locally trivial after restriction to formal Zariski neighborhoods of every point of $U\cap\|D\|$. The same assertions hold for $B^p\subset R^p$.
  - Explanation: Regularity of the completed chart permits extension of a Laurent line across the divisor, and formal basic neighborhoods provide the required local trivializations.
- **claim-laurent-picard-divisibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-picard-divisibility)): Multiplication by $p$ on $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(\|D\|,\mathcal R)$ is surjective.
  - Explanation: Cartier-fixed connections descend locally to horizontal lines over characteristic powers, and their compatible formal trivializations glue to a global root.
- **claim-laurent-unit-orders-and-picard-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-laurent-unit-orders)): Let $\iota_\alpha:D_\alpha% \mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=21AA}% \lhook\joinrel\rightarrow% \EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}% }% \|D\|$ be the inclusion of a prime component. There is an exact sequence of abelian sheaves $$1\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal O}^{\,\times} \longrightarrow\mathcal R^\times \xrightarrow{\ \operatorname{ord}\ } \bigoplus_\alpha(\iota_\alpha)_*\mathbb Z \longrightarrow0. \label{eq:laurent-unit-orders}$$ Consequently restriction gives a surjection $$\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(\|D\|,\widehat{\mathcal O})\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(\|D\|,\mathcal R),$$ whose kernel is generated by the completed restrictions of $\mathcal O_V(D_\alpha)$.
  - Explanation: Laurent units factor into formal units and integral component orders. The order sheaves give surjective Picard restriction with kernel generated by the divisor components.
- **claim-positive-degree-contradicts-divisibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-laurent-degree-contradiction)): The prepared cycle of Proposition <a href="#prop:prepared-cycle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:prepared-cycle">2</a> cannot satisfy the conclusions of Corollary <a href="#cor:form-vanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:form-vanishing">22</a>.
  - Explanation: Intersection degree descends to the Laurent Picard group and is positive on the polarization. Divisibility forces every such integral degree to vanish, giving the contradiction.

        "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subset T_V$ be a saturated bracket-closed and $p$-closed subsheaf of rank $d$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-page-calculus",
        "exact_quote": "The operation $x\\mapsto\\partial x$ induces maps $$d_s:E_s^{i,a}(E)\\longrightarrow E_s^{i-s,a+1}(E)$$ whose cohomology is the next page. At page one, $E_1^{i,a}(E)=H^a(V,P_i^1(E))$; every page has only rows $a=0,1,2$. In particular, with $\\delta_s^a(i)=g_s^a(i;E)-g_{s+1}^a(i;E)$ for $a=0,2$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n g_s^0(i;E)\n &=\\dim\\mathop{\\mathrm{im}}\\bigl(H^0(P_i^s(E))\\longrightarrow H^0(P_i^1(E))\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:bottom-endpoint}\\\\\n g_s^2(i;E)&=g_s^0(1-i;E^!),\\label{eq:top-endpoint}\\\\\n g_{s+1}^1(i;E)\n &=g_s^1(i;E)-\\delta_s^0(i+s)-\\delta_s^2(i-s).\n \\label{eq:middle-recurrence}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $M>0$ and $\\mathcal O(MD)|_{sD}$ is trivial, the page-$s$ ranks are periodic in $i$ modulo $M$, and $$\\label{eq:periodic-euler}\n \\sum_{i\\bmod M}\\bigl(g_s^0(i;E)-g_s^1(i;E)+g_s^2(i;E)\\bigr)\n =\\sum_{i\\bmod M}\\chi(P_i^1(E)).$$ Finally, at each fixed weight $i$, the stable image in Equation <a href=\"#eq:bottom-endpoint\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:bottom-endpoint\">[eq:bottom-endpoint]</a> is precisely the image of the global sections of the completed bundle $\\widehat{E(iD)}$ in $H^0(P_i^1(E))$.",
        "source_id": "PM-035.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-page-calculus",
          "exact_quote": "The operation $x\\mapsto\\partial x$ induces maps $$d_s:E_s^{i,a}(E)\\longrightarrow E_s^{i-s,a+1}(E)$$ whose cohomology is the next page. At page one, $E_1^{i,a}(E)=H^a(V,P_i^1(E))$; every page has only rows $a=0,1,2$. In particular, with $\\delta_s^a(i)=g_s^a(i;E)-g_{s+1}^a(i;E)$ for $a=0,2$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n g_s^0(i;E)\n &=\\dim\\mathop{\\mathrm{im}}\\bigl(H^0(P_i^s(E))\\longrightarrow H^0(P_i^1(E))\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:bottom-endpoint}\\\\\n g_s^2(i;E)&=g_s^0(1-i;E^!),\\label{eq:top-endpoint}\\\\\n g_{s+1}^1(i;E)\n &=g_s^1(i;E)-\\delta_s^0(i+s)-\\delta_s^2(i-s).\n \\label{eq:middle-recurrence}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $M>0$ and $\\mathcal O(MD)|_{sD}$ is trivial, the page-$s$ ranks are periodic in $i$ modulo $M$, and $$\\label{eq:periodic-euler}\n \\sum_{i\\bmod M}\\bigl(g_s^0(i;E)-g_s^1(i;E)+g_s^2(i;E)\\bigr)\n =\\sum_{i\\bmod M}\\chi(P_i^1(E)).$$ Finally, at each fixed weight $i$, the stable image in Equation <a href=\"#eq:bottom-endpoint\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:bottom-endpoint\">[eq:bottom-endpoint]</a> is precisely the image of the global sections of the completed bundle $\\widehat{E(iD)}$ in $H^0(P_i^1(E))$.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The operation $x\\mapsto\\partial x$ induces maps $$d_s:E_s^{i,a}(E)\\longrightarrow E_s^{i-s,a+1}(E)$$ whose cohomology is the next page.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-jets",
        "exact_quote": "Let $u<i$ be integers and let $\\xi\\in H^0(\\mathcal O(iD)/\\mathcal O(uD))$ be nonzero. There is an integer $t$ with $u<t\\leq i$ such that local lifts of $\\xi$ have pole order exactly $tm_\\alpha$ at every $D_\\alpha$. Those lifts give a trivializing section of $\\mathcal O(tD)$ on $(t-u)D$. In particular, $$\\label{eq:scalar-endpoint}\n g_s^0(i;\\mathcal O_V)=\n \\begin{cases}\n 1,&\\mathcal O(iD)|_{sD}\\simeq\\mathcal O_{sD},\\\\\n 0,&\\text{otherwise}.\n \\end{cases}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-035.2"
      },

Those lifts give a trivializing section of $\\mathcal O(tD)$ on $(t-u)D$. In particular, $$\\label{eq:scalar-endpoint}\n g_s^0(i;\\mathcal O_V)=\n \\begin{cases}\n 1,&\\mathcal O(iD)|_{sD}\\simeq\\mathcal O_{sD},\\\\\n 0,&\\text{otherwise}.\n \\end{cases}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"

        "exact_quote": "For the prepared data and periods fixed above, let $E$ be a vector bundle on $V$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\chi(P_i^1(E))=0$ for every integer $i$.*\n\n2.  *For $J=E,E^!$, the spaces $H^0(V,J(nD)/J)$ have dimensions bounded independently of the positive integer $n$.*\n\n3.  *For $J=E,E^!$ there is a constant $A_J$ such that $$g^0_{u_e}(i;J)>0\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(i,M_e\\mathbb Z)\\le A_J$$ for all sufficiently large $e$.*\n\n*Then global sections of $E_{\\mathcal R}$ form a finite-dimensional $k$-vector space.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-laurent-cartier-algebra",
        "exact_quote": "On a smooth affine chart with étale coordinates $x_1,x_2,x_3$ and a local equation $f$ for $D$, put $R=\\widehat A[1/f]$. The monomials $x^I=x_1^{I_1}x_2^{I_2}x_3^{I_3}$, $0\\le I_j<p$, form a basis of $R$ over $R^p$. Ordinary differentials and the Cartier isomorphism extend to this ring. The algebraic bundles $B^1,Z^1$ and their defining exact sequences extend to the corresponding Laurent exact and closed forms, with their Frobenius module structure. In particular, $$\\label{eq:cartier-sequences}\n 0\\longrightarrow\\mathcal O_V\\longrightarrow F_*\\mathcal O_V\n   \\longrightarrow B^1\\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad\n 0\\longrightarrow B^1\\longrightarrow Z^1\n   \\xrightarrow{C}\\Omega\\longrightarrow0$$ are sequences of vector bundles and remain exact on Laurent charts. The images and kernels in the Frobenius de Rham complex are locally free, as are the relevant quotients. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:frobenius-duality}\n (F_*\\mathcal O_V)^\\vee\\otimes\\omega_V\\simeq F_*\\omega_V.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-035.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-laurent-cartier-algebra",

The algebraic bundles $B^1,Z^1$ and their defining exact sequences extend to the corresponding Laurent exact and closed forms, with their Frobenius module structure. In particular, $$\\label{eq:cartier-sequences}\n 0\\longrightarrow\\mathcal O_V\\longrightarrow F_*\\mathcal O_V\n   \\longrightarrow B^1\\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad\n 0\\longrightarrow B^1\\longrightarrow Z^1\n   \\xrightarrow{C}\\Omega\\longrightarrow0$$ are sequences of vector bundles and remain exact on Laurent charts. The images and kernels in the Frobenius de Rham complex are locally free, as are the relevant quotients. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:frobenius-duality}\n (F_*\\mathcal O_V)^\\vee\\otimes\\omega_V\\simeq F_*\\omega_V.$$",

There exist a smooth projective threefold $V$, a generically finite separable morphism $\\rho:V\\to X$, a very ample divisor $H$ on $V$, and a nonzero effective Cartier divisor $$D=\\sum_{\\alpha}m_\\alpha D_\\alpha,\\qquad\n m_\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{>0},\\qquad \\gcd_\\alpha m_\\alpha=1,$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The support of $D$ is connected and has simple normal crossings. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Nef dimension and maximal nef dimension** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For a nef divisor $L$, its nef dimension $n(L)$ is the dimension of its nef reduction; in particular, $n(L)=\dim X$ means that no $L$-trivial curve passes through a very general point.
- **Dualizing twist of a vector bundle** ([source](#pm-h-5)): If $E$ is a vector bundle on a smooth threefold $V$, write $E^!=E^\vee\otimes\omega_V$.
- **Jet width** ([source](#pm-h-5)): The term *width* refers to the number of successive Cartier layers in a jet.
- **Nef-product degree and slope** ([source](#pm-h-16)): For a torsion-free coherent sheaf $E$ of positive rank, set $$\deg(E)=c_1(E)HD,\qquad \mu(E)=\frac{\deg(E)}{\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(E)}.$$
- **Strong semistability** ([source](#pm-h-16)): A sheaf is strongly semistable if all its iterated absolute Frobenius pullbacks are semistable.
- **Laurent sheaf on the completed divisor neighborhood** ([source](#pm-h-22)): Write $\widehat{\mathcal O}_V$ for the completion along $D$, regarded as a sheaf on $\|D\|$, and set $$\mathcal R=\widehat{\mathcal O}_V(*D).$$
- **Very general point** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Here *very general* means outside a countable union of proper closed subsets.
- **Line bundle on the Laurent ringed space** ([source](#eq:laurent-cartier-sequence)): Here and throughout this section, a line bundle means a locally free module of rank one for the indicated sheaf of rings, in this Zariski topology.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-characteristic-threshold | p | > | $3$ | Characteristic in the abundance theorem. |
| q-canonical-kodaira-dimension | \kappa(X,K_X) | = | $1$ | Numerical dimension fixed by the main theorem. |
| q-exceptional-self-intersection | G_i^2 | < | $0$ | Exceptional integral curves of the specified nontrivial regular resolution. |
| q-line-section-bound | h^0(V,L) | ≤ | $1$ | Line bundles of nonpositive degree. |
| q-semistable-hom-dimension | \dim_k\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(G_1,G_2) | ≤ | $\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(G_1)\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(G_2)$ | The specified pair of torsion-free semistable sheaves. |
| q-twisted-bundle-sections | h^0\bigl(V,G(iD)\bigr) | ≤ | $\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}G$ | All integer twists of the fixed semistable bundle. |
| q-tensor-square-sections | h^0\bigl(V,G^{\otimes2}(iD)\bigr) | ≤ | $(\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}G)^2$ | Tensor square of the fixed bundle under arbitrary integer twists; semistability of the tensor square is not assumed. |
| q-scalar-polar-parts | H^0\bigl(V,\mathcal O_V(iD)/\mathcal O_V\bigr) | = | $0$ | Every positive cut. |
| q-limiting-scalar-width | a_* | = | $\frac{p}{p+1}$ | Limit as the characteristic-power periods grow. |
| q-chern-intersection-vanishing | Dc_2(V) | = | $0$ | Chern intersection forced by the scalar page calculation. |
| q-canonical-width-comparison | d'_e | ≤ | $d_e+C_0$ | Sufficiently large periods; the comparison constant is fixed by the supported canonical divisor. |
| q-rank-one-operator-power | (\partial+a)^p | = | $a^p+\partial^{p-1}a$ | Derivations with vanishing characteristic power. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-exceptional-intersections** [claim](#stmt-lem-exceptional-intersections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exceptional-intersections): Use connectedness of the exceptional fiber and the divisor of a local function, then select a component where a coefficient ratio is maximal. -> Exceptional intersection negativity.
- **step-terminal-regularity** [claim](#stmt-lem-terminal-codimension-two) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-terminal-codimension-two): Localize to a surface, contract negative canonical curves while preserving regularity, and contradict terminal discrepancies if exceptional curves remain. -> Regularity in codimension two and isolated singularities.
- **step-full-cycle-torsion** [claim](#stmt-lem-full-cycle-torsion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-full-cycle-torsion): Kill the local-cohomology comparison by a thickening, glue characteristic powers away from the exceptional points, and extend a nowhere-vanishing section. -> Torsion on the full nonreduced Cartier cycle.
- **step-prepared-cycle** [claim](#stmt-prop-prepared-cycle) / [proof](#sec:preparation): Apply nonvanishing and relative birational modifications, use reduced-boundary surface abundance, then take a tame cover, primitive division, and numerical proportionality. -> Smooth prepared threefold and primitive connected nef Cartier cycle with full-cycle torsion.
- **step-connection-destabilization** [claim](#stmt-lem-connection-destabilization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-connection-destabilization): Show that a connection-invariant generic subspace descends through Frobenius and would contradict semistability. -> Nonzero connection-induced destabilization morphism.
- **step-destabilizing-foliation** [claim](#stmt-lem-destabilizing-foliation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-destabilizing-foliation): Use rank constraints, slope separation, and the connection obstruction to annihilate bracket and characteristic-power obstruction maps. -> Bracket-closed and characteristic-power-closed distribution.
- **step-foliation-quotient** [claim](#stmt-lem-foliation-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-foliation-quotient): Glue invariant subrings between the ring and its characteristic powers, construct local coordinate presentations, and take determinants of the cotangent sequence. -> Finite inseparable quotient and canonical bundle formula.
- **step-strong-semistability** [claim](#stmt-prop-strong-semistability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-strong-semistability): Use bend-and-break on the foliation quotient to contradict the exclusion of trivial-degree curves, then apply the connection criterion and Bogomolov inequality. -> Strong tangent semistability and nonnegative Chern intersection.
- **step-line-section-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-degree-zero-line-sections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-degree-zero-line-sections): Turn independent sections into a pencil and use the surface Hodge index theorem to produce forbidden trivial-degree curves. -> Uniform line-section bound.
- **step-hom-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-semistable-hom-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-semistable-hom-bound): Bound morphisms between stable factors using determinant sections and extend the estimate through stable-factor filtrations. -> Hom dimension bounded by the product of ranks.
- **step-polar-bounds** [claim](#stmt-prop-polar-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-polar-bounds): Express bundle sections as Hom spaces, use the cohomology sequence for polar parts, and stabilize the increasing finite-dimensional spaces. -> Uniform section bounds and bounded actual pole orders.
- **step-no-scalar-poles** [claim](#stmt-cor-no-scalar-poles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-no-scalar-poles): Raise a hypothetical nonzero scalar polar part to iterated characteristic powers, contradicting bounded actual pole orders. -> Vanishing of scalar polar parts.
- **step-page-calculus** [claim](#stmt-lem-page-calculus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-page-calculus): Compute page kernels and images, use Cartier presentations and Serre duality for the endpoints, and stabilize finite-dimensional restriction images. -> Page recurrence, endpoint duality, periodic Euler identity, and completed-section realization.
- **step-scalar-jet-rule** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-jets): Detect pole ratios by component valuations, propagate nonvanishing across connected support, and use primitive multiplicities to obtain an integral ratio. -> Uniform integral scalar pole ratio and scalar endpoint classification.
- **step-forced-canonical-jets** [claim](#stmt-lem-forced-canonical-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-forced-canonical-jets): Assume the canonical endpoint eventually vanishes and compare the incompatible middle-row residues forced by consecutive scalar drops. -> Canonical leading jets of arbitrarily large width.
- **step-canonical-formal-root** [claim](#stmt-prop-canonical-formal-root) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-canonical-formal-root): Power canonical jets into scalar jets, force divisibility through component orders, and choose compatible units in stabilized restriction images. -> Integral supported divisor representing the completed canonical line.
- **step-limiting-width** [claim](#stmt-prop-scalar-widths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-scalar-widths): Compare scalar and canonical endpoint drops as multisets, saturate the Euler rank count, and use the swapped residue matching across periods. -> Exact limiting scalar-width ratio and vanishing Chern intersection.
- **step-phase-concentration** [claim](#stmt-lem-phase-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-phase-bound): Center jet weights modulo a period and convert an unbounded displacement into polar parts, using the permitted tensor powers for negative displacements. -> Uniform concentration near the period lattice.
- **step-laurent-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-thm-laurent-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-laurent-vanishing): Stabilize endpoint patterns, control drop lengths, identify the middle distribution, and bound primitive source cuts before taking a convergent sum. -> Finite-dimensional Laurent sections and actual Laurent primitives after refinement.
- **step-laurent-cartier-algebra** [claim](#stmt-lem-laurent-cartier-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-laurent-cartier-algebra): Carry the coordinate basis through completion and localization and compute the Frobenius de Rham complex and its perfect top-form pairing. -> Laurent Cartier exact sequences, local freeness, and Frobenius duality.
- **step-form-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-cor-form-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-form-vanishing): Check the Laurent criterion for cotangent and exact-form bundles using semistability, Riemann–Roch, Frobenius inclusions, and a precision margin; solve closed-form cocycles through Cartier. -> Primitives for ordinary, exact, and closed form cocycles and finite-dimensional global one-forms.
- **step-global-cartier-adjustment** [claim](#stmt-lem-cartier-adjustment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cartier-adjustment): Lift global forms through Cartier using exact-form cocycle vanishing, compare finite dimensions, and prove surjectivity of the semilinear difference by projective extension. -> Closed global forms, bijective Cartier, and surjective additive adjustment.
- **step-cartier-fixed-connection** [claim](#stmt-lem-cartier-fixed-connection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cartier-fixed-connection): Solve logarithmic transition cocycles by closed local forms and subtract a global form solving the Cartier adjustment equation. -> Integrable connection with Cartier-fixed local forms.
- **step-curvature-calculation** [claim](#stmt-lem-cartier-curvature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cartier-curvature): Compute the Cartier coefficient identity and the characteristic power of a rank-one connection operator; use closedness for commutation. -> Commuting connection operators with vanishing characteristic powers.
- **step-horizontal-expansion** [claim](#stmt-lem-horizontal-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-horizontal-expansion): Build split horizontal projectors, prove the truncated Taylor identity and coefficient uniqueness, and check restriction by scalar extension of the projector. -> Horizontal projective line and compatible scalar-extension isomorphism.
- **step-formal-line-extension** [claim](#stmt-lem-formal-line-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-formal-line-extension): Establish regularity of the completed ring by local completions, extend a line by closure of its Weil divisor, and trivialize on formal basic neighborhoods. -> Line extension and formal Zariski local triviality, including characteristic-power subrings.
- **step-picard-divisibility** [claim](#stmt-prop-picard-divisibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-picard-divisibility): Glue horizontal modules over characteristic powers, use formal local triviality, and transport the descended line through Frobenius. -> Surjectivity of characteristic multiplication on the Laurent Picard group.
- **step-unit-orders** [claim](#stmt-lem-laurent-unit-orders) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-laurent-unit-orders): Factor Laurent units by prime component equations and use the flasque integer-order quotient to compute the Picard restriction kernel. -> Surjective Picard restriction with component-generated kernel.
- **step-degree-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-prop-laurent-degree-contradiction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-laurent-degree-contradiction): Descend the integral intersection degree through the Picard kernel and compare positive polarization degree with infinite characteristic divisibility. -> Contradiction to the hypothetical prepared cycle satisfying form vanishing.
- **step-abundance-and-field-descent** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-laurent-degree-contradiction-2): Exclude maximal nef dimension, apply established abundance in the remaining nef dimensions, and descend independent section dimensions through field extension. -> Independent pluricanonical sections, the stated Kodaira dimension, and semiampleness over the original field.

        }
      ],
      "reason": "The curve-exclusion formulation uses an uncountable algebraically closed extension and very general points; the preparation supplies no finite input encoding or terminating procedure for arbitrary threefolds.",
      "step_id": "step-prepared-cycle"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-strong-semistability",
          "exact_quote": "Over the uncountable field, the countably many resulting nonempty opens in $\\mathcal T$ have a common $k$-point.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The contradiction chooses a parameter point in a countable intersection of nonempty opens over the uncountable field, rather than giving a finite search for the required curve.",
      "step_id": "step-strong-semistability"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-canonical-formal-root",
          "exact_quote": "The surjective maps between stabilized images therefore lift a chosen nonzero section on $D_{\\mathrm{red}}$ compatibly to all thickenings.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "A formal trivialization is obtained from compatible lifts through stabilized finite-dimensional images at all thickenings; no computable stabilization cutoff is supplied.",
      "step_id": "step-canonical-formal-root"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-thm-laurent-vanishing",
          "exact_quote": "Their sum converges in a single bounded-pole completed lattice.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The primitive is a convergent infinite sum in a completed bounded-pole lattice, with eventual thresholds established qualitatively.",
      "step_id": "step-laurent-vanishing"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "Then some positive Cartier multiple of $K_X$ has at least two independent global sections.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The conclusion guarantees a positive pluricanonical multiple but gives no explicit bound for that multiple or general procedure computing its sections.",
      "step_id": "step-abundance-and-field-descent"
    }

  "pm_id": "PM-035.2",
  "schema": "pm.roadmap.v2",
  "steps": [
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-exceptional-intersections",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-exceptional-intersections",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be an excellent normal local surface with closed residue field $l$, and let $g:T\\to P$ be a nontrivial projective regular resolution which is an isomorphism away from the closed point. Write $G_i$ for its exceptional integral curves, and take intersection degrees over $l$. Then $G_i^2<0$ for every $i$. More generally, if an exceptional real divisor $Z=\\sum_i z_iG_i$ has a positive coefficient, there is an index $j$ with $z_j>0$ and $Z\\cdot G_j<0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-exceptional-intersections",
      "inputs": [
        "Excellent normal local surface",
        "Nontrivial regular resolution"
      ],
      "method": "Use connectedness of the exceptional fiber and the divisor of a local function, then select a component where a coefficient ratio is maximal.",
      "output": "Exceptional intersection negativity.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-exceptional-intersections"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-terminal-codimension-two",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-exceptional-intersections"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-terminal-codimension-two",
          "exact_quote": "A terminal threefold in the present setting has only isolated singularities.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-terminal-regularity",
      "inputs": [
        "Terminal discrepancies",
        "Local surface adjunction",
        "Formal functions"
      ],
      "method": "Localize to a surface, contract negative canonical curves while preserving regularity, and contradict terminal discrepancies if exceptional curves remain.",
      "output": "Regularity in codimension two and isolated singularities.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-terminal-codimension-two"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-full-cycle-torsion",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-full-cycle-torsion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a normal projective threefold over an algebraically closed field of characteristic $p>0$, and let $L$ be an effective Cartier divisor. Suppose that $L$ is numerically trivial on every integral component of its support and that $\\mathcal O_Y(L)|_{L_{\\mathrm{red}}\\setminus Z}$ is trivial for a finite set of closed points $Z$. Then $\\mathcal O_L(L)$ is torsion.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-full-cycle-torsion",
      "inputs": [
        "Numerical triviality on components",
        "Triviality on the punctured reduction",
        "Finite-length local cohomology"
      ],
      "method": "Kill the local-cohomology comparison by a thickening, glue characteristic powers away from the exceptional points, and extend a nowhere-vanishing section.",
      "output": "Torsion on the full nonreduced Cartier cycle.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-full-cycle-torsion"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-prepared-cycle",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-exceptional-intersections",
        "step-terminal-regularity",
        "step-full-cycle-torsion"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-prepared-cycle",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $k$ is uncountable and algebraically closed of characteristic $p>3$, and that $X$ is a projective terminal threefold with $K_X$ nef, $\\nu(K_X)=1$, and $n(X,K_X)=3$. There exist a smooth projective threefold $V$, a generically finite separable morphism $\\rho:V\\to X$, a very ample divisor $H$ on $V$, and a nonzero effective Cartier divisor $$D=\\sum_{\\alpha}m_\\alpha D_\\alpha,\\qquad\n m_\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{>0},\\qquad \\gcd_\\alpha m_\\alpha=1,$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The support of $D$ is connected and has simple normal crossings. The divisor $D$ is nef, and $D|_{D_\\alpha}$ is numerically trivial for every $\\alpha$.*\n\n2.  *There is no $D$-trivial curve through a very general point of $V$. Moreover $D\\cdot H^2>0$ and $D^2\\cdot H=0$.*\n\n3.  *The invertible sheaf $\\mathcal O_D(D)$ is torsion. Its order will be denoted by $r$.*\n\n\n4.  *For an integer $b\\geq0$, an open neighborhood $U$ of $|D|$, and integers $b_\\alpha$, there is an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:prepared-canonical}\n     \\omega_V^{\\otimes p^b}|_U\n     \\simeq \\mathcal O_U(B_*),\\qquad B_*=\\sum_\\alpha b_\\alpha D_\\alpha .$$ Consequently $K_V\\cdot D\\cdot A=0$ for every Cartier divisor $A$ on $V$.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-prepared-cycle",
      "inputs": [
        "Hypothetical maximal nef dimension",
        "Terminal projective threefold",
        "Surface abundance and resolution"
      ],
      "method": "Apply nonvanishing and relative birational modifications, use reduced-boundary surface abundance, then take a tame cover, primitive division, and numerical proportionality.",
      "output": "Smooth prepared threefold and primitive connected nef Cartier cycle with full-cycle torsion.",
      "proof_anchor": "sec:preparation"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-connection-destabilization",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-prepared-cycle"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-connection-destabilization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be a semistable torsion-free sheaf. If $F_V^*E$ is unstable and $B$ is its first Harder–Narasimhan term, then the connection induces a nonzero morphism on a big open set $$B\\longrightarrow (F_V^*E/B)\\otimes\\Omega_V^1.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-connection-destabilization",
      "inputs": [
        "Semistable torsion-free sheaf",
        "Canonical connection on its Frobenius pullback"
      ],
      "method": "Show that a connection-invariant generic subspace descends through Frobenius and would contradict semistability.",
      "output": "Nonzero connection-induced destabilization morphism.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-connection-destabilization"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-destabilizing-foliation",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-connection-destabilization"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-destabilizing-foliation",
          "exact_quote": "If $T_V$ is unstable, its first Harder–Narasimhan term $A$ is closed under Lie brackets and under $p$-th powers of derivations.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-destabilizing-foliation",
      "inputs": [
        "First tangent destabilizing term",
        "Nef-product slope calculus"
      ],
      "method": "Use rank constraints, slope separation, and the connection obstruction to annihilate bracket and characteristic-power obstruction maps.",
      "output": "Bracket-closed and characteristic-power-closed distribution.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-destabilizing-foliation"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-foliation-quotient",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-destabilizing-foliation"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-foliation-quotient",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subset T_V$ be a saturated bracket-closed and $p$-closed subsheaf of rank $d$. There is a finite purely inseparable map $\\pi\\colon V\\to W$ to a normal projective variety, with a factorization $$V\\xrightarrow{\\pi}W\\xrightarrow{\\rho}V^{(1)}$$ of relative Frobenius. On open sets whose complements have codimension at least two, the canonical bundles satisfy $$\\omega_V\\simeq\\pi^*\\omega_W\\otimes(\\det A)^{\\otimes(p-1)}.\n  \\label{eq:foliation-canonical}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-foliation-quotient",
      "inputs": [
        "Saturated closed distribution"
      ],
      "method": "Glue invariant subrings between the ring and its characteristic powers, construct local coordinate presentations, and take determinants of the cotangent sequence.",
      "output": "Finite inseparable quotient and canonical bundle formula.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-foliation-quotient"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-strong-semistability",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-prepared-cycle",

        "step-foliation-quotient"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-strong-semistability",
          "exact_quote": "The tangent bundle $T_V$ is strongly semistable for the slope $c_1(-)HD/\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(-)$. Moreover, $$c_2(V)D\\ge0.\n  \\label{eq:c2-nonnegative}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-strong-semistability",
      "inputs": [

        "Connection criterion"

      "method": "Use bend-and-break on the foliation quotient to contradict the exclusion of trivial-degree curves, then apply the connection criterion and Bogomolov inequality.",
      "output": "Strong tangent semistability and nonnegative Chern intersection.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-strong-semistability"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-degree-zero-line-sections",

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-degree-zero-line-sections",
          "exact_quote": "If a line bundle $L$ has $\\deg L\\le0$, then $h^0(V,L)\\le1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-line-section-bound",
      "inputs": [
        "Line bundle of nonpositive degree",
        "Prepared curve-exclusion property"
      ],
      "method": "Turn independent sections into a pencil and use the surface Hodge index theorem to produce forbidden trivial-degree curves.",
      "output": "Uniform line-section bound.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-degree-zero-line-sections"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-semistable-hom-bound",
      "depends_on": [

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-semistable-hom-bound",
          "exact_quote": "If $G_1,G_2$ are torsion-free semistable sheaves of slope zero, then $$\\dim_k\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(G_1,G_2)\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(G_1)\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(G_2).\n  \\label{eq:hom-rank-bound}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-hom-bound",
      "inputs": [
        "Slope-zero semistable sheaves",
        "Line-section bound"
      ],
      "method": "Bound morphisms between stable factors using determinant sections and extend the estimate through stable-factor filtrations.",
      "output": "Hom dimension bounded by the product of ranks.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-semistable-hom-bound"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-polar-bounds",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-hom-bound"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-polar-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a semistable vector bundle of slope zero. For every integer $i$, $$h^0\\bigl(V,G(iD)\\bigr)\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}G,\n  \\qquad\n  h^0\\bigl(V,G^{\\otimes2}(iD)\\bigr)\\le(\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}G)^2.\n  \\label{eq:uniform-bundle-sections}$$ For each fixed bundle $J=G$ or $J=G^{\\otimes2}$, the increasing spaces $$H^0\\bigl(V,J(iD)/J\\bigr),\\qquad i=1,2,\\ldots,\n  \\label{eq:polar-part-system}$$ have bounded dimension and eventually stabilize under their natural injections. In particular their elements have a uniform bound on their actual pole orders along every prime component of $D$. This pole bound may depend on $J$; it is uniform in the cut $i$ and in the chosen section.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-polar-bounds",
      "inputs": [
        "Semistable bundle",
        "Hom bound",
        "Twists preserving slope"
      ],
      "method": "Express bundle sections as Hom spaces, use the cohomology sequence for polar parts, and stabilize the increasing finite-dimensional spaces.",
      "output": "Uniform section bounds and bounded actual pole orders.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-polar-bounds"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-cor-no-scalar-poles",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-polar-bounds"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-no-scalar-poles",
          "exact_quote": "For every $i>0$, $$H^0\\bigl(V,\\mathcal O_V(iD)/\\mathcal O_V\\bigr)=0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-no-scalar-poles",
      "inputs": [
        "Scalar specialization of polar bounds"
      ],
      "method": "Raise a hypothetical nonzero scalar polar part to iterated characteristic powers, contradicting bounded actual pole orders.",
      "output": "Vanishing of scalar polar parts.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-cor-no-scalar-poles"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-page-calculus",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-prepared-cycle"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-page-calculus",
          "exact_quote": "The operation $x\\mapsto\\partial x$ induces maps $$d_s:E_s^{i,a}(E)\\longrightarrow E_s^{i-s,a+1}(E)$$ whose cohomology is the next page. At page one, $E_1^{i,a}(E)=H^a(V,P_i^1(E))$; every page has only rows $a=0,1,2$. In particular, with $\\delta_s^a(i)=g_s^a(i;E)-g_{s+1}^a(i;E)$ for $a=0,2$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n g_s^0(i;E)\n &=\\dim\\mathop{\\mathrm{im}}\\bigl(H^0(P_i^s(E))\\longrightarrow H^0(P_i^1(E))\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:bottom-endpoint}\\\\\n g_s^2(i;E)&=g_s^0(1-i;E^!),\\label{eq:top-endpoint}\\\\\n g_{s+1}^1(i;E)\n &=g_s^1(i;E)-\\delta_s^0(i+s)-\\delta_s^2(i-s).\n \\label{eq:middle-recurrence}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $M>0$ and $\\mathcal O(MD)|_{sD}$ is trivial, the page-$s$ ranks are periodic in $i$ modulo $M$, and $$\\label{eq:periodic-euler}\n \\sum_{i\\bmod M}\\bigl(g_s^0(i;E)-g_s^1(i;E)+g_s^2(i;E)\\bigr)\n =\\sum_{i\\bmod M}\\chi(P_i^1(E)).$$ Finally, at each fixed weight $i$, the stable image in Equation <a href=\"#eq:bottom-endpoint\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:bottom-endpoint\">[eq:bottom-endpoint]</a> is precisely the image of the global sections of the completed bundle $\\widehat{E(iD)}$ in $H^0(P_i^1(E))$.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-page-calculus",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite adapted affine cover",
        "Completed pole filtration",
        "Finite Cartier quotients"
      ],
      "method": "Compute page kernels and images, use Cartier presentations and Serre duality for the endpoints, and stabilize finite-dimensional restriction images.",
      "output": "Page recurrence, endpoint duality, periodic Euler identity, and completed-section realization.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-page-calculus"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-jets",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-prepared-cycle",
        "step-page-calculus"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-jets",
          "exact_quote": "Let $u<i$ be integers and let $\\xi\\in H^0(\\mathcal O(iD)/\\mathcal O(uD))$ be nonzero. There is an integer $t$ with $u<t\\leq i$ such that local lifts of $\\xi$ have pole order exactly $tm_\\alpha$ at every $D_\\alpha$. Those lifts give a trivializing section of $\\mathcal O(tD)$ on $(t-u)D$. In particular, $$\\label{eq:scalar-endpoint}\n g_s^0(i;\\mathcal O_V)=\n \\begin{cases}\n 1,&\\mathcal O(iD)|_{sD}\\simeq\\mathcal O_{sD},\\\\\n 0,&\\text{otherwise}.\n \\end{cases}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-scalar-jet-rule",
      "inputs": [
        "Primitive connected cycle",
        "Numerically trivial component restrictions"
      ],
      "method": "Detect pole ratios by component valuations, propagate nonvanishing across connected support, and use primitive multiplicities to obtain an integral ratio.",
      "output": "Uniform integral scalar pole ratio and scalar endpoint classification.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-scalar-jets"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-forced-canonical-jets",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-strong-semistability",
        "step-no-scalar-poles",
        "step-page-calculus",
        "step-scalar-jet-rule"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-forced-canonical-jets",
          "exact_quote": "There are nonzero leading jets of $\\omega_V$ of arbitrarily large width, allowing their upper weight to vary.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-forced-canonical-jets",
      "inputs": [
        "Scalar last-width classification",
        "Periodic Euler identity",
        "Nonnegative Chern intersection"
      ],
      "method": "Assume the canonical endpoint eventually vanishes and compare the incompatible middle-row residues forced by consecutive scalar drops.",
      "output": "Canonical leading jets of arbitrarily large width.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-forced-canonical-jets"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-canonical-formal-root",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-forced-canonical-jets",
        "step-scalar-jet-rule",
        "step-page-calculus"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-canonical-formal-root",
          "exact_quote": "Every coefficient of $B_*$ in Equation <a href=\"#eq:canonical-p-power\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:canonical-p-power\">[eq:canonical-p-power]</a> is divisible by $p^b$. Writing $B_*=p^bB_0$ with $B_0$ integral, there is an isomorphism on the completion along $D$, $$\\label{eq:canonical-formal-root}\n \\widehat{\\omega}_V\\simeq\\widehat{\\mathcal O}_V(B_0).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-canonical-formal-root",
      "inputs": [
        "Wide canonical jets",
        "Local canonical characteristic-power relation",
        "Scalar jet rule"

      "method": "Power canonical jets into scalar jets, force divisibility through component orders, and choose compatible units in stabilized restriction images.",
      "output": "Integral supported divisor representing the completed canonical line.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-canonical-formal-root"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar-widths",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-canonical-formal-root",
        "step-page-calculus",
        "step-scalar-jet-rule"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar-widths",
          "exact_quote": "The scalar widths satisfy $$\\label{eq:limiting-width}\n a_*=\\lim_{e\\to\\infty}\\frac{d_e}{M_e}\n =\\frac{p}{p+1},\\qquad Dc_2(V)=0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-limiting-width",
      "inputs": [
        "Canonical formal root",
        "Scalar and canonical last widths",
        "Middle-row recurrence"
      ],
      "method": "Compare scalar and canonical endpoint drops as multisets, saturate the Euler rank count, and use the swapped residue matching across periods.",
      "output": "Exact limiting scalar-width ratio and vanishing Chern intersection.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-scalar-widths"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-phase-bound",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-polar-bounds",
        "step-limiting-width"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-phase-bound",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a vector bundle that is semistable of slope zero for the degree $c_1(-)\\cdot H\\cdot D$. There is a constant $A_G$ such that, for every sufficiently large $e$, $$g^0_{\\lfloor c_0M_e\\rfloor}(i;G)>0\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(i,M_e\\mathbb Z)\\le A_G.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-phase-concentration",
      "inputs": [
        "Semistable bundle",
        "Uniform polar bounds",
        "Admissible phase constants"
      ],
      "method": "Center jet weights modulo a period and convert an unbounded displacement into polar parts, using the permitted tensor powers for negative displacements.",
      "output": "Uniform concentration near the period lattice.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-phase-bound"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-laurent-vanishing",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-page-calculus",
        "step-limiting-width",
        "step-phase-concentration"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-laurent-vanishing",
          "exact_quote": "For the prepared data and periods fixed above, let $E$ be a vector bundle on $V$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\chi(P_i^1(E))=0$ for every integer $i$.*\n\n2.  *For $J=E,E^!$, the spaces $H^0(V,J(nD)/J)$ have dimensions bounded independently of the positive integer $n$.*\n\n3.  *For $J=E,E^!$ there is a constant $A_J$ such that $$g^0_{u_e}(i;J)>0\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(i,M_e\\mathbb Z)\\le A_J$$ for all sufficiently large $e$.*\n\n*Then global sections of $E_{\\mathcal R}$ form a finite-dimensional $k$-vector space. Every Laurent Čech $1$-cocycle on a finite affine cover adapted to the bundles and the divisor has an actual Laurent primitive. The same assertion holds after refining such a cover.",
          "source_id": "PM-035.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-laurent-vanishing",
      "inputs": [
        "Euler identity",
        "Polar bounds for the bundle and dualizing twist",
        "Leading-jet concentration",
        "Prepared periods"
      ],
      "method": "Stabilize endpoint patterns, control drop lengths, identify the middle distribution, and bound primitive source cuts before taking a convergent sum.",
      "output": "Finite-dimensional Laurent sections and actual Laurent primitives after refinement.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-thm-laurent-vanishing"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-laurent-cartier-algebra",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-prepared-cycle"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-laurent-cartier-algebra",
          "exact_quote": "On a smooth affine chart with étale coordinates $x_1,x_2,x_3$ and a local equation $f$ for $D$, put $R=\\widehat A[1/f]$. The monomials $x^I=x_1^{I_1}x_2^{I_2}x_3^{I_3}$, $0\\le I_j<p$, form a basis of $R$ over $R^p$. Ordinary differentials and the Cartier isomorphism extend to this ring. The algebraic bundles $B^1,Z^1$ and their defining exact sequences extend to the corresponding Laurent exact and closed forms, with their Frobenius module structure. In particular, $$\\label{eq:cartier-sequences}\n 0\\longrightarrow\\mathcal O_V\\longrightarrow F_*\\mathcal O_V\n   \\longrightarrow B^1\\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad\n 0\\longrightarrow B^1\\longrightarrow Z^1\n   \\xrightarrow{C}\\Omega\\longrightarrow0$$ are sequences of vector bundles and remain exact on Laurent charts. The images and kernels in the Frobenius de Rham complex are locally free, as are the relevant quotients. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:frobenius-duality}\n (F_*\\mathcal O_V)^\\vee\\otimes\\omega_V\\simeq F_*\\omega_V.$$",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-abundance-scope**: Under the stated geometric, characteristic, nefness, and numerical-dimension hypotheses, a positive Cartier multiple of the canonical divisor has independent global sections, and the canonical divisor is semiample.
- **invariant-uniform-polar-control**: For each fixed bundle covered by the polar-part proposition, the spaces of polar parts eventually stabilize. Their actual pole orders are bounded independently of the cut and the section; the bound may depend on the bundle.
- **invariant-scalar-width-limit**: In the hypothetical prepared-cycle configuration, the normalized scalar widths have the stated limiting ratio, and the intersection with the second Chern class vanishes.
- **invariant-laurent-primitive-convergence**: The Laurent vanishing criterion gives finite-dimensional global sections and actual Laurent primitives on adapted finite affine covers and their refinements. The primitive must converge inside a single completed lattice with bounded poles.
- **invariant-horizontal-descent**: Commuting connection operators satisfying the specified nilpotence and commutator relations yield a finite projective horizontal module of rank one over the power subring. Scalar extension recovers the original module, compatibly with restriction to smaller Laurent charts retaining the coordinates.
- **invariant-degree-divisibility-obstruction**: In the hypothetical prepared-cycle configuration, the integral degree descends to the Laurent Picard group and is positive on the polarization. Surjectivity of multiplication by the characteristic would force every integral degree to vanish, giving the contradiction.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-unchecked-geometric-hypotheses**: Infer semiampleness for an arbitrary projective variety with a nef canonical divisor. -> The abundance conclusion is established only under the theorem's dimension, singularity, field, factoriality, and numerical-dimension assumptions.
- **anti-use-reduced-cycle-substitution**: Replace torsion on the full Cartier cycle by torsion only on its reduction. -> The later jet calculation uses the nonreduced cycle and its nilpotents; the reduced assertion alone does not supply the required preparation interface.
- **anti-use-fixed-weight-vanishing-alone**: Declare Laurent cocycles solved as soon as their fixed-weight page entries eventually vanish. -> Successive primitives can have uncontrolled poles. The proof separately bounds their source cuts and proves convergence in a single completed lattice.
- **anti-use-linear-cartier-replacement**: Treat Cartier as an ordinary linear map for the usual scalar structures. -> The scalar structure is inverse-Frobenius-semilinear, and the proof distinguishes it from the Frobenius module structure when applying the cohomology calculation.
- **anti-use-local-root-without-topology**: Infer global Picard divisibility solely from horizontal modules on individual Laurent charts. -> A finite projective horizontal module must also be locally trivial in the required formal Zariski topology, and the local constructions must commute with restriction before they glue.
- **anti-use-degree-before-descent**: Apply the integral divisibility obstruction directly to a degree defined only on the completion. -> Picard divisibility concerns Laurent line bundles. The degree must first annihilate the kernel of restriction from the completion.
- **anti-use-finite-jets-as-global-certificate**: Treat agreement on finitely many jet widths as a certificate of the formal canonical root or of abundance. -> The proof passes through arbitrarily large thickenings, compatible inverse limits, and convergent Laurent primitives. A bounded collection of finite computations does not decide those assertions.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The abundance theorem and its formal-neighborhood argument do not supply a finite decision procedure for the stated geometric inputs. Although the horizontal projector is an explicit finite operator formula, it acts on modules over completed Laurent rings and does not replace the global extension, gluing, and convergence arguments. The formal canonical root requires compatible sections through unbounded thickenings: “The surjective maps between stabilized images therefore lift a chosen nonzero section on $D_{\mathrm{red}}$ compatibly to all thickenings.” The Laurent primitive likewise requires completion: “Their sum converges in a single bounded-pole completed lattice.” Finite tests of truncated rings or bounded widths would check a restricted auxiliary calculation rather than certify these global conclusions.

There exist a smooth projective threefold $V$, a generically finite separable morphism $\\rho:V\\to X$, a very ample divisor $H$ on $V$, and a nonzero effective Cartier divisor $$D=\\sum_{\\alpha}m_\\alpha D_\\alpha,\\qquad\n m_\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{>0},\\qquad \\gcd_\\alpha m_\\alpha=1,$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The support of $D$ is connected and has simple normal crossings. The divisor $D$ is nef, and $D|_{D_\\alpha}$ is numerically trivial for every $\\alpha$.*\n\n2.  *There is no $D$-trivial curve through a very general point of $V$. Moreover $D\\cdot H^2>0$ and $D^2\\cdot H=0$.*\n\n3.  *The invertible sheaf $\\mathcal O_D(D)$ is torsion. Its order will be denoted by $r$.*\n\n\n4.  *For an integer $b\\geq0$, an open neighborhood $U$ of $|D|$, and integers $b_\\alpha$, there is an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:prepared-canonical}\n     \\omega_V^{\\otimes p^b}|_U\n     \\simeq \\mathcal O_U(B_*),\\qquad B_*=\\sum_\\alpha b_\\alpha D_\\alpha .$$ Consequently $K_V\\cdot D\\cdot A=0$ for every Cartier divisor $A$ on $V$.",
            "source_object": "stmt-prop-prepared-cycle"
          },
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-cor-form-vanishing",
            "exact_quote": "Every Laurent Čech $1$-cocycle with values in $\\Omega$, $B^1$, or $Z^1$ has a Laurent primitive. Global Laurent one-forms form a finite-dimensional $k$-vector space.",
            "source_object": "stmt-cor-form-vanishing"
          }
        ],

        "quantifiers": [

            "exact_quote": "On the other hand, Proposition <a href=\"#prop:picard-divisibility\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:picard-divisibility\">28</a> implies that every class in this Picard group is divisible by $p^n$ for every positive integer $n$.",
            "source_object": "proof-stmt-prop-laurent-degree-contradiction"
          }
        ],
        "regime": "The same Picard group of locally free Laurent modules in the formal Zariski topology is used for both divisibility and degree.",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Semiampleness is established through a geometric existence proof; the paper supplies no executable procedure for finding a globally generated canonical multiple from a representation of a threefold. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The purely inseparable quotient concerns algebraic foliations in positive characteristic and supplies no evolution rule for a simulated world. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Laurent vanishing criterion guarantees primitives under geometric hypotheses but does not provide a terminating tool for computing them from finite input. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The polar-part spaces eventually stabilize, but no computable stabilization threshold or running-time bound is supplied. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The page spaces and their differentials describe cohomological relationships rather than a finite storage representation with supported operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The horizontal expansion is an identity over Laurent rings and their power subrings; it specifies no hardware arithmetic representation or acceleration architecture. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Surjectivity of multiplication on the Laurent Picard group is a geometric divisibility statement, with no cryptographic construction or computational hardness claim. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Strong semistability concerns tangent bundles under Frobenius pullback and does not establish stability of learned models or training procedures. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The limiting scalar width is derived from cohomological rank constraints; the paper formulates no optimization objective or feasible-solution algorithm. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Restriction-compatible horizontal modules glue over algebraic charts, but this compatibility provides no protocol for communicating processes or handling failures. |
| other (algebraic_geometry) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The theorem supplies a geometric construction: a globally generated canonical multiple defines a morphism whose Stein factorization has connected fibers over a normal projective curve. This is a mathematical existence contract; effective computation of the multiple and sections is ABSENT. |

Suppose that $X$ has terminal singularities and that $K_X$ is nef with $\\nu(K_X)=1$. Then some positive Cartier multiple of $K_X$ has at least two independent global sections. In particular, $\\kappa(X,K_X)=1$ and $K_X$ is semiample.",
          "source_object": "stmt-thm-main"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "sec:introduction",
          "exact_quote": "Geometrically, the theorem gives a morphism $f:X\\to C$ with connected fibers to a normal projective curve and an ample Cartier divisor $A$ on $C$ such that $mK_X\\sim f^*A$ for some $m>0$.",
          "source_object": "sec:introduction"
        }
      ],
      "space_complexity": null,
      "strength": "strong",
      "time_complexity": null,
      "verification": "PENDING",
      "why": "The theorem supplies a geometric construction: a globally generated canonical multiple defines a morphism whose Stein factorization has connected fibers over a normal projective curve. This is a mathematical existence contract; effective computation of the multiple and sections is ABSENT."
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-035.2",
  "schema": "pm.domains.v2"
}
