<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-geometric-and-field-scope** (hypothesis): The abundance conclusion requires the stated normality, projectivity, rational factoriality, dimension, algebraic closedness, and characteristic restriction. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to a variety or ground field outside its stated scope.']
- **limit-terminal-singularities** (hypothesis): Terminal singularities are required; the main theorem does not establish the same conclusion for arbitrary singularities. Blocked use: ['Replacing terminality by a weaker singularity assumption without a separate result.']
- **limit-nef-canonical-divisor** (hypothesis): The canonical divisor must be nef before invoking the abundance conclusion. Blocked use: ['Inferring semiampleness directly for a canonical divisor whose nefness is unestablished.']
- **limit-prescribed-numerical-dimension** (hypothesis): The main theorem treats the specified numerical dimension; it does not assert abundance in every numerical dimension. Blocked use: ['Transferring the conclusion to a different numerical-dimension regime.']
- **limit-unspecified-canonical-multiple** (nonconstructive): The main statement guarantees a positive Cartier multiple with independent sections but specifies no bound for that multiple or procedure for finding its sections. Blocked use: ['Claiming an explicit pluricanonical index, section-finding algorithm, or computational complexity from the theorem alone.']
- **limit-hypothetical-prepared-branch** (formal_scope): The prepared cycle belongs to the hypothetical maximal-nef-dimension branch. Its subsequent incompatible properties are used to exclude that branch. Blocked use: ['Presenting the prepared configuration and all later conclusions as an independently realizable geometric example.']
- **limit-very-general-point-selection** (nonconstructive): The curve-exclusion formulation uses an uncountable algebraically closed field and very general points; it is not a finite sample criterion. Blocked use: ['Certifying the absence of trivial-degree curves by checking only a finite collection of points.']
- **limit-local-surface-resolution** (hypothesis): Exceptional intersection negativity is asserted for the specified nontrivial projective regular resolution of an excellent normal local surface, with intersection degrees taken over its closed residue field. Blocked use: ['Applying the intersection assertion to an arbitrary collection of curves or silently changing the field used for degrees.']
- **limit-full-cycle-torsion-inputs** (hypothesis): The torsion extension requires numerical triviality on every integral support component and triviality on the reduced boundary outside finitely many closed points. Blocked use: ['Inferring full-cycle torsion from reduced-boundary information without the numerical and exceptional-set hypotheses.']
- **limit-reduction-does-not-replace-cycle** (formal_scope): The preparation requires torsion on the entire Cartier cycle, including its nilpotents. Blocked use: ['Substituting the reduced support for the nonreduced cycle in the jet calculation.']
- **limit-prepared-nef-slope** (hypothesis): The semistability and section estimates use the fixed nef-product slope and the prepared numerical and curve-exclusion properties. Blocked use: ['Treating the section bounds as assertions for an arbitrary polarization or arbitrary projective threefold.']
- **limit-fixed-bundle-pole-bound** (formal_scope): The pole bound is uniform in the cut and section for each fixed bundle, but may depend on the bundle. Blocked use: ['Using a single pole bound while changing the bundle through an unrestricted family.']
- **limit-tensor-square-semistability** (formal_scope): The tensor-square section estimate does not establish semistability of arbitrary tensor products. Blocked use: ['Promoting the tensor-square section bound to a general tensor-product semistability theorem.']
- **limit-scalar-jet-primitivity** (hypothesis): The integral scalar pole ratio uses primitive multiplicities, while propagation of nonvanishing uses connected support and numerical triviality on components. Blocked use: ['Applying the integral pole-ratio rule to a disconnected or nonprimitive divisor without re-establishing its conclusion.']
- **limit-formal-root-compatible-lifts** (nonconstructive): The formal canonical root requires compatible units through arbitrarily large thickenings; unrelated trivializations at finitely many widths do not establish it. Blocked use: ['Certifying the completed canonical isomorphism from a bounded collection of jet computations.']
- **limit-laurent-criterion-antecedent** (hypothesis): Laurent vanishing requires the prepared data, the Euler identity, bounded polar parts, and leading-jet concentration, with the paired conditions checked for the bundle and its dualizing twist. Blocked use: ['Asserting Laurent cohomology vanishing for an arbitrary vector bundle.', 'Checking the paired conditions only for the original bundle.']
- **limit-eventual-jet-concentration** (formal_scope): The phase bound holds for sufficiently large periods with a bundle-dependent constant; the statement supplies no universal starting period. Blocked use: ['Treating the concentration implication as valid at every initial period or with a uniform constant for all bundles.']
- **limit-bounded-pole-primitive-convergence** (nonconstructive): Fixed-weight page vanishing alone does not control the poles of successive primitives. The proof separately bounds source cuts and takes a convergent sum in a completed lattice. Blocked use: ['Declaring an actual Laurent primitive solely from eventual vanishing at each weight.', 'Replacing completion by an unproved finite truncation.']
- **limit-cartier-scalar-structures** (formal_scope): Cartier has the stated inverse-Frobenius-semilinear action for ordinary scalars; these scalar structures must be distinguished from the Frobenius-module structures. Blocked use: ['Replacing Cartier by an ordinary linear map when using the cohomology and adjustment arguments.']
- **limit-horizontal-descent-inputs** (hypothesis): Horizontal expansion assumes the specified Laurent chart rings, a free line module, and commuting connection operators satisfying the stated relations. Blocked use: ['Applying the projector descent to arbitrary modules or connections without checking the nilpotence and commutator relations.']
- **limit-formal-topology-for-gluing** (formal_scope): Local horizontal projective modules must be locally trivial in the formal Zariski topology and compatible with restriction before they define the global descended line. Blocked use: ['Inferring global Picard divisibility from isolated chartwise roots without a gluing argument.']
- **limit-integral-degree-must-descend** (formal_scope): The integral degree must descend from the completion to the same Laurent Picard group on which divisibility is proved. Blocked use: ['Applying the integral divisibility obstruction before proving that the degree annihilates the Picard restriction kernel.']
- **limit-original-field-descent** (formal_scope): The field-extension reduction does not assume preservation of rational factoriality. The final section statement uses a Cartier multiple compatible with the original index and proper section-space base change. Blocked use: ['Assuming rational factoriality survives arbitrary field extension.', 'Transferring sections back to the original field without the Cartier-index and base-change argument.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let <i>X</i> be a projective ℚ-factorial terminal threefold over an algebraically closed field of characteristic <i>p</i> &gt; 3. We prove the numerical-dimension-one case of abundance: if <i>K</i><sub><i>X</i></sub> is nef with $`\nu(K_X)=1`$, then <i>K</i><sub><i>X</i></sub> is semiample and $`\kappa(X,K_X)=1`$.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The abundance conjecture predicts that a nef canonical divisor on a projective variety with mild singularities is semiample: some positive Cartier multiple is generated by its global sections. Such sections turn numerical information about the canonical divisor into a morphism that records the canonical geometry of the variety. For a nef divisor $L$ on a threefold, numerical dimension one means $L\cdot H^2>0$ and $L^2\cdot H=0$ for an ample Cartier divisor $H$. We prove this case of abundance for terminal threefolds in characteristic greater than three.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $X$ be a normal projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial threefold over an algebraically closed field $k$ of characteristic $p>3$. Suppose that $X$ has terminal singularities and that $K_X$ is nef with $\nu(K_X)=1$. Then some positive Cartier multiple of $K_X$ has at least two independent global sections. In particular, $\kappa(X,K_X)=1$ and $K_X$ is semiample.*

</div>

No assumption is made on the first Betti number, irregularity, or Albanese variety. Thus Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> resolves the numerical-dimension-one case of the abundance conjecture under the stated terminal and characteristic hypotheses.

Geometrically, the theorem gives a morphism $f:X\to C$ with connected fibers to a normal projective curve and an ample Cartier divisor $A$ on $C$ such that $mK_X\sim f^*A$ for some $m>0$. Indeed, the image of a morphism defined by a globally generated pluricanonical multiple is a curve, since $\nu(K_X)=1$. Its Stein factorization gives $f$; the finite pullback to $C$ of a hyperplane divisor on the image gives $A$.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context

Over the complex numbers, Miyaoka proved the numerical-dimension-one case of threefold abundance by studying effective pluricanonical divisors, primitive connected cycles, and their infinitesimal neighborhoods (Miyaoka 1988) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[miyaoka1988](#ref-miyaoka1988)]. Kawamata’s abundance theorem for minimal threefolds includes another proof of this case using the log minimal model program and formal thickenings (Kawamata 1992, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[kawamata1992](#ref-kawamata1992)]. The present argument develops this canonical-cycle framework in positive characteristic. More recently, Liu–Xu developed higher-dimensional numerical-dimension-one abundance over $\mathbb C$ under nonvanishing hypotheses (Liu and Xu 2025) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[liuxu2025](#ref-liuxu2025)]. The ground field in the present theorem requires the positive-characteristic inputs described next.

The positive-characteristic minimal model program supplies the birational operations needed to study abundance for threefolds. Over algebraically closed fields of characteristic greater than five, Hacon–Xu established standard-coefficient flips and minimal models for projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal threefolds with pseudoeffective canonical divisor (C. D. Hacon and Xu 2015, Theorems 1.1 and 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[haconxu2015](#ref-haconxu2015)]. Birkar proved flips for $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt threefold pairs and log minimal models for projective klt threefold pairs with pseudoeffective log canonical divisor (Birkar 2016, Theorems 1.1 and 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[birkar2016](#ref-birkar2016)]. The relative log minimal model program in characteristic greater than five was established by Hashizume, Nakamura and Tanaka (Hashizume, Nakamura, and Tanaka 2020) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[hnt2020](#ref-hnt2020)]; Hacon and Witaszek treated the characteristic-five case (C. Hacon and Witaszek 2022) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[hw2022](#ref-hw2022)].

In characteristic greater than five, Xu–Zhang proved nonvanishing for terminal minimal threefolds (C. Xu and Zhang 2019) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[xuzhang2019](#ref-xuzhang2019)]; Witaszek obtained nonvanishing for klt threefold pairs and abundance in nef dimension at most two through a canonical bundle formula (Witaszek 2021) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[witaszek2021](#ref-witaszek2021)]. Zhang proved abundance for minimal threefolds with nontrivial Albanese map (Zhang 2020) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[zhang2020](#ref-zhang2020)]. Xu proved nonvanishing for pseudoeffective log canonical threefold pairs in characteristic greater than three and semiampleness when the nef log canonical divisor has positive Kodaira dimension (Z. Xu 2024, Theorems 1.4 and 1.6) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[xu2024](#ref-xu2024)]. His theorem for nef dimension at most two (Z. Xu 2024, Theorem 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[xu2024](#ref-xu2024)] and his subsequent numerical-dimension-two abundance theorem (Z. Xu 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[xu2026](#ref-xu2026)] leave a specific issue in the present setting: an effective canonical divisor of numerical dimension one could a priori still have Kodaira dimension zero and maximal nef dimension. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> excludes that possibility. Here nef dimension is the dimension of the nef reduction, and maximal nef dimension means that no curve of canonical degree zero passes through a very general point. It is a different invariant from numerical dimension. The new step is the production of a second pluricanonical section; the existence of a first section and the passage from positive Kodaira dimension to semiampleness are Xu’s results.

Our tangent-sheaf argument follows the positive-characteristic foliation method appearing in Xu–Zhang (C. Xu and Zhang 2019, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[xuzhang2019](#ref-xuzhang2019)]. It uses the Bogomolov inequality for strongly semistable sheaves with respect to a nef tuple, in Langer’s formulation (Langer 2004b, Theorem 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[langer2004](#ref-langer2004)]; the corrections in (Langer 2004a) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[langeradd2004](#ref-langeradd2004)] preserve that theorem. Surface abundance enters through abundance for projective semi-log-canonical surfaces, proved by Tanaka over algebraically closed fields (Tanaka 2016, Theorem 0.1) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[tanaka2016](#ref-tanaka2016)] and by Posva over arbitrary fields (Posva 2023, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[posva2024](#ref-posva2024)].

The descent mechanism is classical Cartier theory. In particular, Katz’s proof of Cartier descent supplies the horizontal projector and truncated Taylor expansion (Katz 1970, Theorem 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[katz1970](#ref-katz1970)]. We establish the completed-ring and bounded-pole statements that allow this algebra to be used on the punctured formal neighborhood of a nonreduced cycle. The three-row jet calculation and its uniform pole control are the bridge between the canonical-cycle geometry and this descent argument.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The argument

After nonvanishing and the birational preparation in Section <a href="#sec:preparation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preparation">2</a>, the hypothetical maximal-nef-dimension case produces a smooth projective threefold $V$ and a connected effective nef Cartier cycle $D$ with primitive multiplicities. The cycle is numerically trivial on each of its components, its normal line $\mathcal O_D(D)$ is torsion, and, on a neighborhood of $D$, a $p$-power of $\omega_V$ has a divisor supported on $D$. The preparation proves torsion on the full nonreduced cycle, which is needed in the subsequent calculation.

Section <a href="#sec:slopes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:slopes">3</a> then proves strong semistability of the tangent and cotangent bundles for the nef degree $c_1(-)\cdot H\cdot D$. The foliation quotient of a destabilizing sheaf would produce $D$-trivial curves through very general points. The resulting semistability gives $c_2(V)\cdot D\geq0$ and uniform bounds on polar parts, which are the inputs to the jet calculation.

The central calculation in Section <a href="#sec:jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:jets">4</a> concerns finite jets along $D$, that is, sections on successive Cartier thickenings. It keeps all three cohomological rows of the pole filtration and compares their ranks over the periods $rp^e$, where $r$ is the order of $\mathcal O_D(D)$. The scalar rows identify the completed canonical line with the line of an integral divisor supported on $D$, and force both $c_2(V)\cdot D=0$ and the precise limiting ratio $p/(p+1)$ for the last trivialization widths. These facts control the positions and lengths of differentials for vector bundles as well.

Section <a href="#sec:laurent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:laurent">5</a> turns the calculation into a formal-neighborhood criterion, Theorem <a href="#thm:laurent-vanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:laurent-vanishing">20</a>: a bundle satisfying an Euler identity, bounded polar parts, and a concentration condition for leading jets has vanishing first Laurent cohomology. A bound on the source cuts of incoming differentials ensures that the constructed primitives actually converge with bounded poles. We prove this criterion directly for finite covers and completed lattices. The criterion applies to one-forms and exact forms.

Section <a href="#sec:cartier" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cartier">6</a> uses these vanishings to make every line bundle on the punctured formal neighborhood a $p$th power, by Cartier descent. On the other hand, intersection with $H$ on the components of $D$ defines a nonzero integer-valued homomorphism on its Picard group. Such a homomorphism cannot exist on a $p$-divisible group. The contradiction proves Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

Figure <a href="#fig:proof-map" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-map">1</a> records the two conclusions about the same Picard group that give the contradiction. In the figure, $\mathcal R=\widehat{\mathcal O}_V(*D)$ denotes functions on the completion along $D$ with a finite pole bound; its Picard group consists of locally free rank-one $\mathcal R$-modules on $|D|$.

<figure id="fig:proof-map">

<figcaption>The maximal-nef-dimension assumption produces a cycle for which Laurent cohomology forces Picard divisibility, while intersection theory supplies a nonzero integral degree.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-proof-map"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:proof-map`)

Visual witness description of PDF page 3 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
Flowchart diagram showing the contradiction argument. At the top is the root node "Prepared cycle $(V, D)$". From this node, two branches diverge: the left downward arrow is labeled "Sections 3–5" leading to the node "Slope bounds and finite jets \ Laurent cocycles have primitives"; below this, an arrow labeled "Cartier descent" points downward to the node "$\mathrm{Pic}(|D|, \mathcal{R})$ \ is $p$-divisible". The right downward arrow from the root is labeled "Section 6" leading to the node "Orders of Laurent units \ Intersection with $H$"; below this, an arrow points downward to the node "$\Phi \colon \mathrm{Pic}(|D|, \mathcal{R}) \to \mathbb{Z}$ \ $\Phi(\mathcal{O}(H)) = DH^2 > 0$". Downward arrows from both the left bottom node and right bottom node converge at a terminal node labeled "Contradiction".
```


All arguments take place in characteristic $p$. The proof uses no lifting to characteristic zero and no orbifold Iitaka theorem.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Conventions

Varieties are integral and all divisors are rational Cartier divisors when a pullback or intersection requires this, unless they are explicitly declared integral Cartier divisors. On a smooth variety we identify Cartier and Weil divisors. Linear and numerical equivalence are denoted by $\sim$ and $\equiv$, with a subscript $\mathbb Q$ for rational linear equivalence. For a nef divisor $L$, its nef dimension $n(L)$ is the dimension of its nef reduction; in particular, $n(L)=\dim X$ means that no $L$-trivial curve passes through a very general point. We use an uncountable algebraically closed field when making this formulation, and descend the final section-space statement to the original field.

If $E$ is a vector bundle on a smooth threefold $V$, write $E^!=E^\vee\otimes\omega_V$. All completions below are along an effective Cartier divisor, and all Laurent sheaves are considered on its Zariski support. The term *width* refers to the number of successive Cartier layers in a jet.

<a id="sec:preparation"></a>
### Birational preparation

The purpose of this section is to replace the hypothetical case of maximal nef dimension by a smooth threefold carrying a single connected nef cycle. The torsion assertion below concerns the entire Cartier cycle, with its nilpotents.

<a id="stmt-prop-prepared-cycle"></a>
<div id="prop:prepared-cycle" class="proposition">

**Proposition 2** (The prepared cycle). *Suppose that $k$ is uncountable and algebraically closed of characteristic $p>3$, and that $X$ is a projective terminal threefold with $K_X$ nef, $\nu(K_X)=1$, and $n(X,K_X)=3$. There exist a smooth projective threefold $V$, a generically finite separable morphism $\rho:V\to X$, a very ample divisor $H$ on $V$, and a nonzero effective Cartier divisor $$D=\sum_{\alpha}m_\alpha D_\alpha,\qquad
 m_\alpha\in\mathbb Z_{>0},\qquad \gcd_\alpha m_\alpha=1,$$ with the following properties.*

1.  *The support of $D$ is connected and has simple normal crossings. The divisor $D$ is nef, and $D|_{D_\alpha}$ is numerically trivial for every $\alpha$.*

2.  *There is no $D$-trivial curve through a very general point of $V$. Moreover $D\cdot H^2>0$ and $D^2\cdot H=0$.*

3.  *The invertible sheaf $\mathcal O_D(D)$ is torsion. Its order will be denoted by $r$.*

<a id="eq:prepared-canonical"></a>
4.  *For an integer $b\geq0$, an open neighborhood $U$ of $|D|$, and integers $b_\alpha$, there is an isomorphism $$\label{eq:prepared-canonical}
     \omega_V^{\otimes p^b}|_U
     \simeq \mathcal O_U(B_*),\qquad B_*=\sum_\alpha b_\alpha D_\alpha .$$ Consequently $K_V\cdot D\cdot A=0$ for every Cartier divisor $A$ on $V$.*

</div>

The case $n(X,K_X)\leq2$ is already covered by (Z. Xu 2024, Theorem 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[xu2024](#ref-xu2024)], so this is the remaining nef-dimension case. The rest of this section proves Proposition <a href="#prop:prepared-cycle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:prepared-cycle">2</a>. Starting with an effective pluricanonical divisor, we isolate a reduced boundary component by the relative minimal model program. Surface adjunction supplies torsion on its reduction; local cohomology and $p$-power gluing then give torsion on the full Cartier cycle. A tame cover and primitive division produce $(V,D)$, and numerical proportionality transfers the maximal-nef-dimension condition to $D$. The intervening local surface arguments establish regularity in codimension two and justify reduced-boundary adjunction. The use of a connected primitive cycle follows the canonical-divisor viewpoint of Miyaoka (Miyaoka 1988, Definition 2.4 and Lemma 2.5) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[miyaoka1988](#ref-miyaoka1988)]; the torsion and covering arguments here take place in characteristic $p$.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Resolution and field extension

We use projective resolution in dimension three (Cossart and Piltant 2008, 2009, 2019) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[cossartpiltant2008](#ref-cossartpiltant2008), [cp2009](#ref-cp2009), [cp2019](#ref-cp2019)]. The projective form that is unchanged over the regular locus is stated explicitly in (Cossart 2008, 1, introductory theorem) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[cossart2008notes](#ref-cossart2008notes)]. It applies to our quasiprojective threefolds over the perfect field $k$. For divisor supports on a regular threefold we use embedded resolution (Cossart, Jannsen, and Saito 2009, Corollary 0.4) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[cjs2009](#ref-cjs2009)]: a reduced closed subscheme of dimension at most two in a regular excellent scheme admits a projective modification, unchanged off that subscheme, whose reduced inverse image is snc. The resolutions may be chosen to be isomorphisms over the specified regular open set. In particular, pairs admit the log resolutions used below.

We also use the discrepancy criterion for a simple normal crossing subpair on a smooth variety, allowing negative coefficients: coefficients at most one give log canonicity, and coefficients strictly less than one give the klt property. One can see the relevant inequalities directly. At the generic point of a center, use logarithmic differentials for the components through it and regular differentials for the other coordinates. At the generic point of a divisor on a smooth model, with uniformizer $t$, each pulled-back logarithmic differential is a regular differential plus a multiple of $dt/t$. Their exterior product has at most a simple pole: two occurrences of $dt/t$ have zero exterior product. The relative Jacobian formula gives the discrepancy inequality. Lowering a coefficient adds its positive difference times the order of that component. For an exceptional divisor whose center misses the boundary, the relative Jacobian has positive order. Indeed a birational morphism between smooth models cannot be generically unramified along an exceptional divisor, since it would then be quasi-finite there. These observations also explain why it suffices to compute thresholds on a log resolution.

We may replace $k$ by an uncountable algebraically closed extension. Here are the descent and preservation points needed for this reduction. A normal variety over an algebraically closed field is geometrically normal and geometrically integral. In the present setting, geometric normality also follows from Serre’s criterion: the field is perfect, residue fields of finite type are separably generated, and the relevant flat base-change maps have regular fibers. A smooth projective resolution remains smooth and integral after extension. The canonical rational line bundle base-changes, since its restriction to the smooth open set does, and reflexive extension determines it elsewhere.

On a resolution $g:T\to X$, terminality gives $$K_T=g^*K_X+\sum_E a_EE,\qquad a_E>0.$$ The base-changed resolution has the same positive exceptional discrepancies. A further exceptional divisor has a positive relative discrepancy over the smooth model and nonnegative contributions from the effective divisor $\sum_Ea_EE$; hence terminality is preserved. Nefness is preserved, for example by writing a nef class as a limit of ample rational classes, and intersection numbers preserve the numerical dimension. Finally, for every Cartier multiple $qK_X$, $$H^0(X_{k'},\mathcal O_{X_{k'}}(qK_{X_{k'}}))
   =H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(qK_X))\otimes_k k'.$$ Thus pluricanonical sections descend in the required sense of equality of dimensions. If necessary, take a further multiple divisible by the original Cartier index; the powers of two linearly independent sections remain linearly independent, since their nonconstant ratio cannot acquire a constant positive power in a function field with algebraically closed constant field. We do not assume that $\mathbb Q$-factoriality is preserved by this extension: the modifications below supply it when needed.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### The maximal nef-dimension branch

Nonvanishing (Z. Xu 2024, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[xu2024](#ref-xu2024)] supplies an effective Cartier divisor $$0\neq N_0\sim mK_X,\qquad m>0.$$ It is nonzero because $K_X\cdot H_X^2>0$ for an ample divisor $H_X$. It is nef, and $N_0^2\cdot H_X=0$. Every prime component $T$ of $N_0$ satisfies $$N_0\cdot H_X\cdot T=0,$$ because these nonnegative numbers have a positive weighted sum equal to zero. In fact $N_0|_T\equiv0$. To verify this for an integral curve $C\subset T$, choose a sufficiently large multiple of $H_X|_T$ with an effective Cartier member containing $C$. Its intersection with the nef restriction is zero; all components contribute nonnegatively, so the contribution of $C$ is zero.

The nef reduction exists over our uncountable algebraically closed field (Z. Xu 2026, Definition 2.1 and Theorem 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[xu2026](#ref-xu2026)]. If $n(X,K_X)\leq2$, the low-nef-dimension abundance theorem (Z. Xu 2024, Theorem 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[xu2024](#ref-xu2024)] makes $K_X$ semiample. Since its numerical dimension is one, some Cartier multiple has at least two sections. We may therefore assume $n(X,K_X)=3$. The defining curve property of the nef reduction says in this case that there is no $N_0$-trivial curve through a very general point of $X$. Here *very general* means outside a countable union of proper closed subsets.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Isolating a boundary component

We use the relative threefold MMP in characteristic greater than three, including preservation of $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt pairs for the boundary driving the MMP, crepant dlt modifications, and the partial MMP with nef restored boundary (Z. Xu 2026, Theorem 2.20, Proposition 2.21, Definition 2.23, Lemma 2.24, and Theorem 2.27) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[xu2026](#ref-xu2026)]. The characteristic-five case is included through (C. Hacon and Witaszek 2022) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[hw2022](#ref-hw2022)]; the corresponding results in characteristic greater than five include (Hashizume, Nakamura, and Tanaka 2020) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[hnt2020](#ref-hnt2020)]. Termination for the pseudoeffective lc divisor we use is (Z. Xu 2024, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[xu2024](#ref-xu2024)]. The comparisons of pullbacks use the negativity lemma (Kollár and Mori 1998, Lemma 3.39) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[kollarMori1998](#ref-kollarMori1998)].

We recall why the dlt preservation used here concerns negative steps. On a common resolution of a negative divisorial contraction or flip, the old log pullback minus the new one is effective by negativity. On curves over the contraction target its degree is nonpositive, and is strictly negative if the curve has a nonconstant image on a modified side. A component of a projective fiber not contained in this effective divisor cannot meet it: its effective restriction is anti-nef, hence zero. Connectedness of fibers then makes any fiber meeting the support lie entirely in it. Fibers over the modification locus do meet the support, by the strict degree inequality. Thus discrepancies strictly increase for valuations centered in that locus. A zero log discrepancy on the output of a dlt step must consequently come from the unchanged open set, where the required simple normal crossing behavior persists. We will apply this to the lowered boundary. For a larger boundary whose log divisor is trivial along the steps we retain log canonicity by crepancy, and later take a new dlt modification.

Let $\lambda>0$ be the log canonical threshold of $N_0$ on $X$. It is finite and rational, and is attained: on a log resolution it is the minimum of finitely many positive rational discrepancy-to-order ratios. Thus $(X,\lambda N_0)$ is lc and is not klt. Take a crepant projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt modification $$\mu:(W,B)\longrightarrow(X,\lambda N_0).$$ All $\mu$-exceptional divisors occur in $B$ with coefficient one. One construction starts on a log resolution with the strict boundary and all exceptional divisors reduced. Its log divisor is the desired pullback plus an effective exceptional discrepancy divisor. The relative dlt MMP removes that difference by negativity, leaving precisely the crepant divisors. This is the modification theorem cited above.

<a id="eq:initial-dlt-pair"></a>
Put $N=\mu^*N_0$ and $\tau=m^{-1}+\lambda>0$. Then $$\label{eq:initial-dlt-pair}
 K_W+B\sim_{\mathbb Q}\tau N,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}B=\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}N.$$ Indeed an extracted log-canonical place must have center in $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}N_0$, since $X$ is klt away from that divisor. Its order on $N_0$ is positive. The modification is an isomorphism over the smooth open set $X_{\mathrm{reg}}\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}N_0$: there it has no exceptional divisors, and a projective small birational morphism to a locally factorial variety is an isomorphism. For the latter assertion, push down a relatively ample Cartier divisor. A multiple of its pushdown is Cartier, and its pullback agrees with that multiple upstairs because there are no exceptional divisors. It therefore has degree zero on any contracted curve, contradicting relative ampleness unless there are no such curves.

Since $(W,B)$ is dlt and not klt, choose a coefficient-one component $S$. Choose a rational $\epsilon>0$ so small that both $$B-\epsilon S\geq0,\qquad \tau N-\epsilon S\geq0.$$ Run a $(K_W+B-\epsilon S)$-MMP which is $(K_W+B)$-trivial. In the partial-MMP notation the boundary is $B-\epsilon S$, and the restoration divisor is $\epsilon S$. The lowered pair is dlt and its log divisor is pseudoeffective by the displayed effective representative. Termination therefore applies. There is no Mori fiber output, since a pseudoeffective divisor cannot be anti-ample on the covering family of curves in a Mori fiber space.

We spell out what happens to $N$. On a common resolution of each step, its two pullbacks agree. Their difference is exceptional over the new model. A curve contracted over that model maps on the old model to a curve over the step’s contraction target, and has $N$-degree zero, because the extremal ray is $N$-trivial. The difference is therefore numerically trivial over the new model, and is zero by negativity applied to it and to its negative. This proves the pullback assertion for flips as well as divisorial contractions.

It follows that the transform of $N$ remains nef and numerically trivial on every component of its support. For the latter assertion, lift a curve on such a component to the common resolution. Its image on the old side is a point or a curve in the old support, where $N$ is numerically trivial. The same pullback equality shows that the full pair in <a href="#eq:initial-dlt-pair" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:initial-dlt-pair">[eq:initial-dlt-pair]</a> remains crepant and lc. The component $S$ is not contracted: every contracted ray $R$ satisfies $$S\cdot R>0,$$ whereas an exceptional divisor of a divisorial contraction has negative degree on some contracted curve, again by negativity. Moreover a negative curve for the effective divisor $\tau N-\epsilon S$ lies in its support. Thus all steps are isomorphisms off the support under consideration.

<a id="eq:isolated-nef-pair"></a>
Write $Y,B_Y,S_Y,N_Y$ for the output and its transforms. The partial-MMP conclusion gives $$\label{eq:isolated-nef-pair}
 K_Y+B_Y\sim_{\mathbb Q}\tau N_Y,\qquad
 K_Y+B_Y-\delta S_Y\ \text{nef}$$ for some rational $\delta>0$. The variety $Y$ is $\mathbb Q$-factorial and $(Y,B_Y)$ is lc. If $T\neq S_Y$ is a component of $N_Y$, then $-S_Y|_T$ is nef by <a href="#eq:isolated-nef-pair" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:isolated-nef-pair">[eq:isolated-nef-pair]</a> and $N_Y|_T\equiv0$. But $S_Y|_T$ is effective and $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. If nonzero, it has positive degree against an ample divisor on the integral projective surface $T$, contradicting anti-nefness. Consequently $$S_Y\cap T=\varnothing\qquad(T\neq S_Y).$$ The pair $(Y,S_Y)$ is lc, and, with $s=\operatorname{mult}_{S_Y}N_Y>0$, <a id="eq:isolated-boundary"></a>$$\label{eq:isolated-boundary}
 S_Y|_{S_Y}\equiv0,\qquad
 K_Y+S_Y\sim_{\mathbb Q}\tau s S_Y
 \quad\text{on a neighborhood of }S_Y .$$

<a id="eq:second-dlt-support"></a>
<a id="eq:dlt-adjunction-input"></a>
Take a new crepant projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt modification $$f:(Y_d,\Sigma)\longrightarrow(Y,S_Y),$$ where $\Sigma$ is the strict transform of $S_Y$ together with the exceptional divisors, all reduced. We claim $$\label{eq:second-dlt-support}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}f^*S_Y=\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\Sigma .$$ For an extracted divisor $E$, let $a_E$ denote log discrepancy. Log canonicity gives $$0\leq a_E(Y,B_Y)
 =a_E(Y,S_Y)-\operatorname{ord}_E(B_Y-S_Y)
 =-\operatorname{ord}_E(B_Y-S_Y).$$ Both terms on the right must vanish. Crepancy with $(X,\lambda N_0)$ then gives $a_E(X,\lambda N_0)=0$; since $X$ is klt, $\operatorname{ord}_E N_0>0$. The pullback equalities just proved give $\operatorname{ord}_E N_Y>0$. The other components of $N_Y$ have the same support as $B_Y-S_Y$ and order zero at $E$, so $\operatorname{ord}_E S_Y>0$. This proves <a href="#eq:second-dlt-support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:second-dlt-support">[eq:second-dlt-support]</a>. The morphism $f$ is an isomorphism off $S_Y$, by the small-morphism argument and $\mathbb Q$-factoriality of $Y$. Near $\Sigma$ we therefore have $$\label{eq:dlt-adjunction-input}
 K_{Y_d}+\Sigma\sim_{\mathbb Q}\tau s f^*S_Y,\qquad
 f^*S_Y|_\Sigma\equiv0 .$$

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Local surface intersections and terminal regularity

The adjunction calculation for $\Sigma$ uses an intersection inequality on excellent local surfaces, whose residue fields need not be perfect. We prove that inequality first and also derive regularity of $X$ in codimension two. For a resolution unchanged over $X_{\mathrm{reg}}\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}N_0$, this regularity ensures that exceptional divisors with centers outside $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}N_0$ are disjoint from its total pullback.

<a id="stmt-lem-exceptional-intersections"></a>
<div id="lem:exceptional-intersections" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Exceptional surface intersections). *Let $P$ be an excellent normal local surface with closed residue field $l$, and let $g:T\to P$ be a nontrivial projective regular resolution which is an isomorphism away from the closed point. Write $G_i$ for its exceptional integral curves, and take intersection degrees over $l$. Then $G_i^2<0$ for every $i$. More generally, if an exceptional real divisor $Z=\sum_i z_iG_i$ has a positive coefficient, there is an index $j$ with $z_j>0$ and $Z\cdot G_j<0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-exceptional-intersections"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-exceptional-intersections</summary>

*Proof.* The exceptional fiber is connected, by proper birationality and normality of $P$. Choose a nonzero nonunit $t\in\mathcal O_P$ and write $$\operatorname{div}_T(t)=A+\sum_i c_iG_i,\qquad c_i>0,$$ where $A$ is the strict-transform part. It meets the exceptional fiber: the strict transform of a component of the divisor of $t$ has a point over the closed point by properness. For $C=\sum_i c_iG_i$ we have $$C\cdot G_i=-A\cdot G_i\leq0.$$ Distinct curves have nonnegative intersections. Connectedness therefore shows $G_i^2<0$: every vertex has either a neighbor or, if it is the only vertex, a positive intersection with $A$.

For the second assertion put $a=\max_i(z_i/c_i)>0$, and let $I$ be the nonempty set where this maximum is attained. Choose $j\in I$ which either meets $A$ or has a neighbor outside $I$. Such a vertex exists by connectedness and the fact that $A$ meets the fiber. Then $$Z\cdot G_j
 =aC\cdot G_j+\sum_i(z_i-ac_i)G_i\cdot G_j<0.$$ The term with $i=j$ is zero; all other terms are nonpositive, and the chosen vertex makes at least one term strictly negative. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-terminal-codimension-two"></a>
<div id="lem:terminal-codimension-two" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Terminal threefolds are regular in codimension two). *A terminal threefold in the present setting has only isolated singularities.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-terminal-codimension-two"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-terminal-codimension-two</summary>

*Proof.* Localize at a codimension-two point. This gives an excellent normal local surface $P$, with residue field $l$, and a projective regular resolution $g:T\to P$ obtained by localizing a threefold resolution. The residue field $l$ need not be perfect. We keep all intersection degrees over $l$.

<a id="eq:local-surface-adjunction"></a>
For an integral exceptional curve $G$, divisor adjunction and curve duality give $$\label{eq:local-surface-adjunction}
 (K_T+G)\cdot G
 =2\dim_lH^1(G,\mathcal O_G)-2\dim_lH^0(G,\mathcal O_G).$$ To specify the duality in this formula, use the dualizing complex on $P$ localized from the threefold and its exceptional inverse image on $T$. Transitivity for $G\to T\to P$ and $G\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}l\to P$ identifies the adjunction sheaf on $G$ with its dualizing sheaf over $l$, up to tensoring with a one-dimensional $l$-vector space. This does not change degree. Proper duality and the formula $\chi(\mathcal L)-\chi(\mathcal O_G)=\deg_l\mathcal L$ prove <a href="#eq:local-surface-adjunction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-surface-adjunction">[eq:local-surface-adjunction]</a> (Hartshorne 1966, 1977).

Suppose $K_T\cdot G<0$, and set $l'=H^0(G,\mathcal O_G)$ and $d=[l':l]$. Both $G^2$ and $K_T\cdot G$ are negative multiples of $d$. Equation <a href="#eq:local-surface-adjunction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-surface-adjunction">[eq:local-surface-adjunction]</a> and Lemma <a href="#lem:exceptional-intersections" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:exceptional-intersections">3</a> force $$H^1(G,\mathcal O_G)=0,\qquad G^2=K_T\cdot G=-d.$$ The line bundle $\mathcal O_G(-G)$ consequently has degree one over $l'$. Riemann–Roch gives at least two independent sections over $l'$. Every nonzero section has a zero scheme of length one. Two independent sections have no common zero: otherwise their effective Cartier divisors coincide, so their ratio is a global unit, an element of $l'^\times$. They define a nonconstant map $G\to\mathbb P^1_{l'}$ of degree one. It is finite, and a finite birational morphism to a normal curve is an isomorphism. Hence $$G\simeq\mathbb P^1_{l'},\qquad \mathcal O_G(G)\simeq\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^1_{l'}}(-1).$$

For completeness this curve can be contracted while retaining a projective regular resolution over $P$. Choose a relatively very ample Cartier divisor $A$, put $a=\deg_{l'}(A|_G)>0$, and set $Q=A+aG$. The divisor $Q-G$ has positive degree on every component of the closed fiber, and is relatively ample by the fiber criterion for ampleness (Grothendieck 1961) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[egaIII](#ref-egaIII)]. The restriction $Q|_G$ is trivial. For every $n\geq1$, a trivializing section of $\mathcal O_G(nQ)$ extends successively to $nG$, because the obstruction groups are $$H^1\bigl(G,\mathcal O_G(nQ-jG)\bigr)
   =H^1(\mathbb P^1_{l'},\mathcal O(j))=0\qquad(j\geq1).$$ For large $n$, relative Serre vanishing applied to $n(Q-G)$ lifts these sections from $nG$ to $T$. They generate along $G$. Away from $G$, the sections of $n(Q-G)$, multiplied by the canonical section of $nG$, generate and separate points and tangent directions. Thus $nQ$ defines a projective contraction whose only nontrivial fiber is $G$. Its Stein factorization has normal target and is an isomorphism off $G$.

Let $q$ be the image of $G$. Formal functions identifies the completed local ring at $q$ with $$\varprojlim_n H^0(nG,\mathcal O_{nG}).$$ The $G$-adic and inverse-image $q$-adic neighborhoods are cofinal, since they have the same support. The successive kernels have graded pieces $$H^0(G,\mathcal O_G(-nG))=H^0(\mathbb P^1_{l'},\mathcal O(n)).$$ There is no obstruction in $H^1$, so the associated graded algebra is the polynomial ring $l'[x,y]$. More explicitly, let $F_n$ be the kernel of the map from the completed local ring to $H^0(nG,\mathcal O_{nG})$. Then $F_1$ is its maximal ideal, and the $F_n$-filtration is cofinal with the maximal-ideal-adic filtration. Choose lifts of the two degree-one generators. Generation of the graded algebra gives $F_1\subset(x,y)+F_n$ for every $n$, by successive approximation, using arbitrary lifts of coefficients from $l'$. Finitely generated ideals in the complete Noetherian local ring are closed, so $F_1=(x,y)$. No embedding of the residue field $l'$ into the completed local ring is required. Its dimension is two; hence it, and then the local ring at $q$, is regular. This proves the claimed contraction using formal functions and Serre vanishing (Hartshorne 1977, III, Sections 5 and 11) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[hartshorne1977](#ref-hartshorne1977)].

There are only finitely many exceptional curves. Contracting a $K_T$-negative one whenever possible therefore yields a regular resolution with $K_T$ relatively nef. If any exceptional curves remain, terminality gives $$E=K_T-g^*K_P=\sum_i a_iG_i,\qquad a_i>0.$$ The discrepancies of surviving curves have not changed, since they are read at their generic points. Lemma <a href="#lem:exceptional-intersections" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:exceptional-intersections">3</a> gives $E\cdot G_j<0$ for some $j$, contradicting relative nefness. There are no exceptional curves left, and the proper birational map is an isomorphism. Thus the codimension-two point was regular. Normality already gives regularity in codimension one, so the remaining singular locus of the projective threefold is finite. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Adjunction on the reduced boundary

We return to the dlt pair $(Y_d,\Sigma)$ satisfying <a href="#eq:dlt-adjunction-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dlt-adjunction-input">[eq:dlt-adjunction-input]</a>. To apply surface abundance, we must identify its restricted log canonical class with that of a projective slc surface.

<a id="eq:slc-adjunction"></a>
Let $\sigma:\Sigma'\to\Sigma$ be the finite $S_2$-ification. It is an isomorphism away from finitely many closed points (Z. Xu 2026, Proposition 2.14) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[xu2026](#ref-xu2026)]. Reduced-boundary adjunction gives $$\label{eq:slc-adjunction}
 (K_{Y_d}+\Sigma)|_{\Sigma'}=K_{\Sigma'}+\Delta',$$ where $(\Sigma',\Delta')$ is an slc surface pair (Z. Xu 2026, Lemma 2.19) <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[xu2026](#ref-xu2026)]. Restrictions here and below mean rational line bundles, or sufficiently divisible Cartier multiples. We give the local verification needed in this dlt situation.

<a id="eq:boundary-log-pullback"></a>
<a id="eq:surface-relative-vanishing-input"></a>
Choose a log resolution and write $$\label{eq:boundary-log-pullback}
 K_{\widetilde Y}+\widetilde\Sigma+\sum_i\beta_iE_i
   =h^*(K_{Y_d}+\Sigma),\qquad \beta_i\leq1.$$ At a codimension-one point of $\Sigma$, localize the ambient threefold to a normal local surface $P$, and write $h:T\to P$ for the localized regular resolution. If the pair is not snc at this point, dlt gives $\beta_i<1$ on the exceptional curves over it. Set $$J=-\sum_i\lfloor\beta_i\rfloor E_i,\qquad
 M=J-\widetilde\Sigma,\qquad
 A_{\mathrm{fr}}=\sum_i\{\beta_i\}E_i.$$ Here $J$ is effective, and $$\label{eq:surface-relative-vanishing-input}
 M-K_T-A_{\mathrm{fr}}\equiv_P0 .$$

We claim $R^1h_*\mathcal O_T(M)=0$. For every nonzero effective integral exceptional cycle $Z$, the divisor $Z-A_{\mathrm{fr}}$ has a positive coefficient. Lemma <a href="#lem:exceptional-intersections" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:exceptional-intersections">3</a> gives a component $E_j$ with positive coefficient in $Z$ such that $(Z-A_{\mathrm{fr}})\cdot E_j<0$. Using <a href="#eq:surface-relative-vanishing-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:surface-relative-vanishing-input">[eq:surface-relative-vanishing-input]</a>, $$(M-Z+E_j)\cdot E_j>(K_T+E_j)\cdot E_j
   =\deg_l\omega_{E_j/l}.$$ Curve duality gives $H^1(E_j,\mathcal O_{E_j}(M-Z+E_j))=0$: the dual space consists of sections of a line bundle of negative degree. The exact sequence $$0\longrightarrow\mathcal O_{E_j}(M-Z+E_j)
 \longrightarrow\mathcal O_Z(M)
 \longrightarrow\mathcal O_{Z-E_j}(M)\longrightarrow0$$ then proves $H^1(Z,\mathcal O_Z(M))=0$ by induction on the sum of the coefficients of $Z$. Exceptional cycles give a cofinal system of neighborhoods of the exceptional fiber. Formal functions, followed by faithful completion for the coherent higher direct image, proves the claim.

Normality gives $h_*\mathcal O_T(J)=\mathcal O_P$: a rational function with possible poles only along exceptional divisors is regular at every codimension-one point of $P$, hence regular on $P$. The preceding vanishing and $$0\longrightarrow\mathcal O_T(M)\longrightarrow\mathcal O_T(J)
 \longrightarrow\mathcal O_{\widetilde\Sigma}(J)\longrightarrow0$$ give a surjection $\mathcal O_P\to h_*\mathcal O_{\widetilde\Sigma}(J)$. It factors through $h_*\mathcal O_{\widetilde\Sigma}$, which is a subsheaf of the target. The strict boundary has no exceptional component, so its proper map to the local boundary is quasi-finite and hence finite. The kernel of $\mathcal O_P\to h_*\mathcal O_{\widetilde\Sigma}$ is precisely the ideal of the reduced boundary. The surjection thus identifies $\widetilde\Sigma$ with that boundary and also gives $\mathcal O_{\widetilde\Sigma}=\mathcal O_{\widetilde\Sigma}(J)$ under their natural inclusion. In particular $J$ misses the strict boundary.

Two strict branches cannot cross above this non-snc point. In a nontrivial regular surface resolution any such intersection over the closed point lies on the exceptional fiber; the total divisor would then have three components through a point on a surface, contrary to snc. The trivial-resolution case would already be snc downstairs. Thus the boundary is regular at a non-snc point of codimension one, and every exceptional coefficient meeting its strict transform is nonnegative. At the snc points, the reduced boundary is nodal. This proves that $\Sigma'$ is nodal in codimension one.

Here is the log-line identification in <a href="#eq:slc-adjunction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:slc-adjunction">[eq:slc-adjunction]</a>. Use rational residue forms to identify the plurilog-canonical line with pluricanonical forms on each boundary component. At snc points, Cartier divisor adjunction gives this identification, including along the nodes. At the other codimension-one points, restrict <a href="#eq:boundary-log-pullback" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:boundary-log-pullback">[eq:boundary-log-pullback]</a> to the smooth strict component. The preceding local calculation identifies it with the boundary there and shows that the resulting different is effective, since the exceptional coefficients meeting it are nonnegative. It has coefficient zero along the nodes. The identification extends by the $S_2$ property, using reflexive powers with the indicated divisorial twist. Its right side is therefore $\mathbb Q$-Cartier with exactly the rational line bundle on the left side of <a href="#eq:slc-adjunction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:slc-adjunction">[eq:slc-adjunction]</a>.

On the normalization of $\Sigma'$, the boundary is the transform of $\Delta'$ together with the conductor, the latter with coefficient one. This is the residue formula at a node, extended from codimension one. Its log pullback to the smooth strict component $\widetilde\Sigma_i$ is $$K_{\widetilde\Sigma_i}
 +\left((\widetilde\Sigma-\widetilde\Sigma_i)
                   +\sum_j\beta_jE_j\right)|_{\widetilde\Sigma_i}.$$ This follows from smooth adjunction applied to <a href="#eq:boundary-log-pullback" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:boundary-log-pullback">[eq:boundary-log-pullback]</a>, with compatible rational residue forms, so it is an equality of log pullbacks, not just numerical classes. The displayed subpair is snc with every coefficient at most one. The discrepancy criterion at the start of the section makes it lc. Thus $\Sigma'$ is $S_2$, nodal in codimension one, and lc after normalization with conductor; this proves the asserted slc property.

<a id="eq:reduced-boundary-triviality"></a>
By <a href="#eq:dlt-adjunction-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dlt-adjunction-input">[eq:dlt-adjunction-input]</a>, the log canonical class in <a href="#eq:slc-adjunction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:slc-adjunction">[eq:slc-adjunction]</a> is numerically trivial. The surface $\Sigma'$ is projective, since it is finite over $\Sigma$. Abundance for projective slc surfaces in positive characteristic (Posva 2023, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[posva2024](#ref-posva2024)] makes this class semiample, hence $\mathbb Q$-linearly trivial. Indeed a globally generated numerically trivial line bundle gives a constant morphism on each connected component and is trivial there. It follows from <a href="#eq:dlt-adjunction-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dlt-adjunction-input">[eq:dlt-adjunction-input]</a>, whose coefficient $\tau s$ is positive rational, that some positive Cartier multiple $L$ of $f^*S_Y$ satisfies $$\label{eq:reduced-boundary-triviality}
 \mathcal O_{Y_d}(L)|_{\Sigma\setminus Z}\simeq\mathcal O_{\Sigma\setminus Z}$$ for a finite set $Z$ of closed points. Its support is $\Sigma$, and $L|_{\Sigma_i}\equiv0$ for every component.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Torsion on the full Cartier cycle

We now pass from <a href="#eq:reduced-boundary-triviality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:reduced-boundary-triviality">[eq:reduced-boundary-triviality]</a> to the actual nonreduced divisor $L$.

<a id="stmt-lem-full-cycle-torsion"></a>
<div id="lem:full-cycle-torsion" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Extending torsion across finitely many points). *Let $Y$ be a normal projective threefold over an algebraically closed field of characteristic $p>0$, and let $L$ be an effective Cartier divisor. Suppose that $L$ is numerically trivial on every integral component of its support and that $\mathcal O_Y(L)|_{L_{\mathrm{red}}\setminus Z}$ is trivial for a finite set of closed points $Z$. Then $\mathcal O_L(L)$ is torsion.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-full-cycle-torsion"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-full-cycle-torsion</summary>

*Proof.* Replacing $Z$ by $Z\cap|L|$, we may assume $Z\subset|L|$. For $z\in Z$, put $R=\mathcal O_{Y,z}$ and let $\mathfrak m$ be its maximal ideal. Normality gives depth at least two, so $$H^1_{\mathfrak m}(R)=0.$$ Every nonmaximal localization of $R$ is a normal local ring of dimension at most two, and is consequently Cohen–Macaulay. This implies that $H^2_{\mathfrak m}(R)$ has finite length. Explicitly, present $R=A/I$, where $A$ is a regular local ring of dimension $n$, essentially of finite type over the field. The height of $I$ is $n-3$. At a nonmaximal prime in $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R$, catenarity and Auslander–Buchsbaum give projective dimension $n-3$ over the corresponding localization of $A$. Hence $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_A^{n-2}(R,A)$ is supported only at the closed point. It is a finite module, thus has finite length. Local duality identifies $H^2_{\mathfrak m}(R)$ with its Matlis dual, up to completion, and proves the assertion (Hartshorne 1967) <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[hartshorne1967](#ref-hartshorne1967)].

<a id="eq:torsion-zero-comparison"></a>
Let $t_z$ be a local equation of $L$. Choose $j$ so large that $t_z^{j-1}$ kills $H^2_{\mathfrak m}(R)$ for every $z\in Z$. There are only finitely many points, so the same $j$ works at all of them. The restriction sequences fit into a commutative diagram $$\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=small]
0 \arrow[r] &
\mathcal O_Y((u-j)L) \arrow[r] \arrow[d] &
\mathcal O_Y(uL) \arrow[r] \arrow[d,equal] &
\mathcal O_{jL}(uL) \arrow[r] \arrow[d] & 0\\
0 \arrow[r] &
\mathcal O_Y((u-1)L) \arrow[r] &
\mathcal O_Y(uL) \arrow[r] &
\mathcal O_L(uL) \arrow[r] & 0 .
\end{tikzcd}$$ In local trivializations the left vertical map is multiplication by $t_z^{j-1}$. The vanishing of the ambient $H^1_Z$ makes the connecting maps from the $H^1_Z$ of the rightmost terms to the ambient $H^2_Z$ injective. The choice of $j$ therefore gives $$\label{eq:torsion-zero-comparison}
 H^1_Z(Y,\mathcal O_{jL}(uL))
       \longrightarrow H^1_Z(Y,\mathcal O_L(uL))
 \quad\text{equal to zero for every integer }u .$$ The assertion is uniform in $u$: twisting by $uL$ only changes local trivializations of these finite-length modules.

Fix this $j$. The kernel of $\mathcal O_{jL\setminus Z}\to\mathcal O_{L_{\mathrm{red}}\setminus Z}$ is nilpotent. Lift a chosen generator of the trivial line $\mathcal O_Y(L)|_{L_{\mathrm{red}}\setminus Z}$ to local generating sections on a finite open cover of $jL\setminus Z$. On overlaps their differences are in the nilpotent ideal. For $u=p^e$ sufficiently large, their $u$-th powers agree, since the $p^e$-power map is additive in characteristic $p$ and kills that ideal. The powers therefore give a trivializing section $$s_j\in H^0(jL\setminus Z,\mathcal O_{jL}(uL)).$$ Project it to a section $s$ on $L\setminus Z$. In the exact sequence of sections with support in $Z$, its obstruction to extending over $L$ is the image of the obstruction for $s_j$. Equation <a href="#eq:torsion-zero-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:torsion-zero-comparison">[eq:torsion-zero-comparison]</a> kills that image. Thus $s$ extends to $H^0(L,\mathcal O_L(uL))$.

This extension is a generator everywhere. On each integral projective surface component $T$ of $L_{\mathrm{red}}$ it is nonzero, since it is a generator off $Z$. If it vanished somewhere on $T$, its zero scheme would be a nonempty effective Cartier divisor: a nonzero section of an invertible sheaf on an integral scheme is a non-zero-divisor in local trivializations. Such a divisor has positive degree against an ample class, whereas $\mathcal O_T(uL)$ is numerically trivial. This is impossible. No normality of $T$ is needed for this intersection argument. Nonvanishing after reduction implies invertibility on $L$, by the local criterion for a unit modulo a nilpotent ideal. We conclude $\mathcal O_L(uL)\simeq\mathcal O_L$, as required. ◻

</details>

Applying Lemma <a href="#lem:full-cycle-torsion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:full-cycle-torsion">5</a> to $Y_d$ and $L$ proves that $\mathcal O_L(L)$ is torsion.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### A tame cover and the primitive cycle

Choose a smooth projective common resolution $V_0$ of $Y_d$ and $X$. It may be chosen to be an isomorphism over the common open set $$X_{\mathrm{reg}}\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}N_0$$ and to resolve the relevant divisor supports. Let $\rho_0:V_0\to X$ be its map to $X$. The pullbacks of $N_Y$ and $N_0$ agree on $V_0$, by the pullback equalities through the MMP.

<a id="eq:pluricanonical-section-divisor"></a>
The section cutting out $N_0\sim mK_X$ gives a rational section $\theta$ of $\omega_{V_0}^{\otimes m}$ with $$\label{eq:pluricanonical-section-divisor}
 \operatorname{div}(\theta)
     =\rho_0^*N_0+m(K_{V_0}-\rho_0^*K_X).$$ Every exceptional center outside $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}N_0$ is an isolated singular point of $X$, by Lemma <a href="#lem:terminal-codimension-two" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:terminal-codimension-two">4</a> and the choice of the resolution. The divisors over those centers are disjoint from $\rho_0^*N_0$. Thus the support in <a href="#eq:pluricanonical-section-divisor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pluricanonical-section-divisor">[eq:pluricanonical-section-divisor]</a> consists of the total pullback support and possibly divisors disjoint from it.

Write $$m=p^bm',\qquad b\geq0,\qquad (m',p)=1.$$ Choose a nonzero rational generator $\eta$ of $\omega_{V_0}^{\otimes p^b}$, so that $\theta=a\eta^{m'}$ for $a\in k(V_0)^\times$. Adjoin one root $v$ of $v^{m'}=a$, and normalize $V_0$ in the resulting field. The extension is finite separable, possibly of degree smaller than $m'$. Its normalization is projective. Away from $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\operatorname{div}(\theta)$, this construction is an integral component of the torsor of $m'$-th roots of a nowhere-vanishing section of a line bundle. It is finite étale there, since $m'$ is invertible in $k$.

Resolve this normalization, and then the total divisor support, keeping that regular étale open set unchanged. Denote the resulting smooth projective variety by $V$, and its generically finite separable map to $V_0$ by $\pi$. The root $v\eta$ is a rational section of $\pi^*\omega_{V_0}^{\otimes p^b}$. The determinant of the differential of $\pi$ is a nonzero rational section of $\omega_V\otimes\pi^*\omega_{V_0}^{-1}$; it is nonzero because $\pi$ is separable. Multiplying $v\eta$ by the $p^b$-th power of this Jacobian section produces a rational section of $\omega_V^{\otimes p^b}$. Its divisor is supported over $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\operatorname{div}(\theta)$, since $\pi$ is étale off that support. In particular it is supported on the total pullback of $N_0$, together with divisors disjoint from it.

Let $L_V$ be the pullback of $L$ from $Y_d$, and choose one connected component $C$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}L_V$. The isolation of $S_Y$ shows that $C$ is disjoint from the remainder of the total pullback support of $N_0$. Write the part of $L_V$ supported on $C$ as $$aD,\qquad
 D=\sum_\alpha m_\alpha D_\alpha,\qquad
 a=\gcd_\alpha\bigl(\operatorname{mult}_{D_\alpha}L_V\bigr).$$ Then $D$ is a nonzero primitive effective Cartier divisor, since $V$ is smooth. Its support is connected and snc by the final resolution.

The pullback $L_V$ is numerically trivial on its support: any curve there maps to a point or to a curve in $\Sigma$, where $L$ is numerically trivial. On a neighborhood of $C$, $L_V=aD$. Consequently $D|_{D_\alpha}\equiv0$. For a curve not contained in $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}D$, effectivity gives nonnegative intersection with $D$; for one contained in a component the degree is zero. Hence $D$ is nef. We have $$D^2\cdot H=0,\qquad D\cdot H^2>0$$ for every very ample divisor $H$.

If $\mathcal O_L(tL)\simeq\mathcal O_L$, its pullback is trivial on the Cartier scheme $L_V$. Near $C$, the inclusion $D\subset aD=L_V$ is an inclusion of closed subschemes. Restricting the pulled-back trivialization gives $$\mathcal O_D(taD)\simeq\mathcal O_D.$$ Thus $\mathcal O_D(D)$ is torsion. This argument does not claim that its order is unchanged under the cover or primitive division.

The rational canonical section constructed above has, on a neighborhood of $C$, divisor supported on $D$; all other components of its divisor are disjoint from $C$. It therefore gives <a href="#eq:prepared-canonical" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prepared-canonical">[eq:prepared-canonical]</a>. For any Cartier divisor $A$ on $V$, $$p^bK_V\cdot D\cdot A
   =B_*\cdot D\cdot A
   =\sum_\alpha b_\alpha\,
       c_1(\mathcal O_{D_\alpha}(D))\cdot A|_{D_\alpha}=0.$$ This includes $K_V^2\cdot D=0$.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### Numerical proportionality and the final interface

<a id="eq:prepared-proportionality"></a>
Put $\rho=\rho_0\circ\pi$ and $N_{\mathrm{tot}}=\rho^*N_0$. Both $D$ and $N_{\mathrm{tot}}$ are nef and nonzero. The latter is numerically trivial on every component of its support: every curve there maps to a point or to a curve in $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}N_0$. We have $$D^2\cdot H=N_{\mathrm{tot}}^2\cdot H
           =D\cdot N_{\mathrm{tot}}\cdot H=0,
 \qquad D\cdot H^2>0,\quad N_{\mathrm{tot}}\cdot H^2>0.$$ The surface Hodge index theorem implies that $$\label{eq:prepared-proportionality}
 D\equiv cN_{\mathrm{tot}},\qquad
 c=\frac{D\cdot H^2}{N_{\mathrm{tot}}\cdot H^2}>0.$$ Here is a way to check the numerical equivalence on every curve rather than just on one general surface. Given an integral curve $C_0\subset V$, take $a\gg0$ and an integral surface $T\in|aH|$ containing $C_0$. Such a surface exists by Bertini irreducibility with this prescribed base locus (Jouanolou 1983) <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[jouanolou1983](#ref-jouanolou1983)]: for sufficiently large $a$, generators of the twisted ideal of $C_0$, multiplied by very ample sections, give a linear system with no fixed divisor and with a birational map to its image off $C_0$. The image has dimension three, so a general member is integral.

On a smooth projective resolution $\widetilde T$ of $T$, the pullbacks of $D$ and $N_{\mathrm{tot}}$ are nef, isotropic, mutually orthogonal classes. The pullback of $H|_T$ has positive square. The Hodge index form on $N^1(\widetilde T)_{\mathbb R}$ has signature $(1,\dim_{\mathbb R}N^1(\widetilde T)_{\mathbb R}-1)$ (Hartshorne 1977, V, Section 1) <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[hartshorne1977](#ref-hartshorne1977)]; its restriction to the orthogonal complement of any positive-square class is negative definite. Two nonzero orthogonal isotropic classes with positive intersection against that class are therefore positively proportional. Their ratio is the number $c$ in <a href="#eq:prepared-proportionality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prepared-proportionality">[eq:prepared-proportionality]</a>, since intersection with the pullback of $H|_T$ gives $aD\cdot H^2$ and $aN_{\mathrm{tot}}\cdot H^2$. Choose a curve on the resolution dominating $C_0$; projection of degrees proves $(D-cN_{\mathrm{tot}})\cdot C_0=0$. This proves <a href="#eq:prepared-proportionality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prepared-proportionality">[eq:prepared-proportionality]</a>.

Finally a very general point of $V$ lies in the generically finite locus of $\rho$ and maps outside the countable union excluded by the nef reduction on $X$. A curve through such a point has a curve as its image. If it were $D$-trivial, <a href="#eq:prepared-proportionality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prepared-proportionality">[eq:prepared-proportionality]</a> and the projection formula would make its image $N_0$-trivial, a contradiction. This establishes the remaining assertion of Proposition <a href="#prop:prepared-cycle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:prepared-cycle">2</a>. The proof of that proposition is complete. In the subsequent sections only $V,D,H$, the torsion order $r$, and the local canonical relation <a href="#eq:prepared-canonical" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prepared-canonical">[eq:prepared-canonical]</a> are retained; the auxiliary birational models are no longer needed.

<a id="sec:slopes"></a>
### Slopes, foliations, and polar parts

<a id="eq:slope-numerical-input"></a>
We retain the smooth projective threefold $V$ and the cycle $D$ of Proposition <a href="#prop:prepared-cycle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:prepared-cycle">2</a>. Fix a very ample divisor $H$ on $V$. The numerical properties used in this section are $$H^2D>0,\qquad HD^2=0,\qquad K_VDA=0
  \quad\text{for every divisor class }A.
  \label{eq:slope-numerical-input}$$ We also use that $D$ is nef and that no curve of $D$-degree zero passes through a very general point of $V$. The ground field is uncountable, algebraically closed, and of characteristic $p>3$. We will obtain strong semistability and $c_2(V)D\geq0$, followed by uniform bounds on vector-valued polar parts and the vanishing of scalar polar parts. The scalar vanishing starts the jet calculation in Section <a href="#sec:jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:jets">4</a>; the vector bounds control Laurent primitives in Section <a href="#sec:laurent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:laurent">5</a>.

<a id="pm-h-16"></a>
#### Slope calculus for the nef product

For a torsion-free coherent sheaf $E$ of positive rank, set $$\deg(E)=c_1(E)HD,\qquad
  \mu(E)=\frac{\deg(E)}{\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(E)}.$$ Stability and semistability will always refer to this slope. A sheaf is strongly semistable if all its iterated absolute Frobenius pullbacks are semistable. Although $D$ need not be ample, the elementary slope calculus needed below still applies. We give the details that are relevant here.

An effective divisor has nonnegative degree, since $H$ and $D$ are nef. Saturating a subsheaf therefore does not decrease its degree. Every torsion-free sheaf $E$ embeds in a finite direct sum of copies of $\mathcal O_V(aH)$ for some $a$: dualize a sufficiently positive global-generation map for $E^\vee$ and use $E%
  \mathrel{%
    \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=21AA}%
    \lhook\joinrel\rightarrow%
    \EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%
  }%
E^{\vee\vee}$. If $B\subset E$ has rank $b$, taking exterior powers and then determinants gives a nonzero map from $\det B$ to some copy of $\mathcal O_V(baH)$. Hence $$\mu(B)\le aH^2D.$$ For ranks bounded by $\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}E$, the possible slopes form a discrete subset of $\mathbb Q$. Thus a maximal slope is attained. Among saturated subsheaves of maximal slope, take one of maximal rank. The usual sum-and-intersection argument shows that this subsheaf contains every other subsheaf of maximal slope. It is semistable, and the maximal slope of its torsion-free quotient is strictly smaller. Repeating on the quotient produces the Harder–Narasimhan filtration.

<a id="eq:slope-hom-vanishing"></a>
Write $\mu_{\max}(E)$ and $\mu_{\min}(E)$ for the largest and smallest slopes of its factors. Additivity of degree and the image of a morphism give the familiar implication $$\mu_{\min}(E)>\mu_{\max}(F)
  \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(E,F)=0.
  \label{eq:slope-hom-vanishing}$$ Indeed a nonzero image is both a torsion-free quotient of the source and a subsheaf of the target, which would force its slope to be at least the left-hand extreme and at most the right-hand extreme. Dualizing the filtration on the open set where its terms and factors are locally free gives $$\mu_{\max}(E^\vee)=-\mu_{\min}(E),\qquad
  \mu_{\min}(E^\vee)=-\mu_{\max}(E).$$ The omitted set has codimension at least two and does not change any determinant or degree. The same observation permits all ensuing bundle calculations on such open sets, with saturation or reflexive extension when returning to $V$.

Tensoring by a line bundle adds its degree to every slope. More generally, the same rule holds for a rank-one torsion-free factor after restriction to the open set where it is invertible. In particular, Equation <a href="#eq:slope-numerical-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:slope-numerical-input">[eq:slope-numerical-input]</a> shows that twisting by $iD$ leaves all slopes unchanged, for every $i\in\mathbb Z$. We will use this rank-one rule; no assertion about semistability of arbitrary tensor products is required.

<a id="pm-h-17"></a>
#### Connections and distributions of rank at most two

Let $F_V\colon V\to V$ denote absolute Frobenius. A Frobenius pullback $F_V^*E$ has its canonical connection, which in a pulled-back local frame differentiates the coefficients. We first record the elementary descent observation behind its use.

<a id="stmt-lem-connection-destabilization"></a>
<div id="lem:connection-destabilization" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6** (The connection detects destabilization). *Let $E$ be a semistable torsion-free sheaf. If $F_V^*E$ is unstable and $B$ is its first Harder–Narasimhan term, then the connection induces a nonzero morphism on a big open set $$B\longrightarrow (F_V^*E/B)\otimes\Omega_V^1.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-connection-destabilization"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-connection-destabilization</summary>

*Proof.* The induced map is $\mathcal O_V$-linear by the Leibniz rule. Suppose it vanishes. Then the generic subspace defined by $B$ is horizontal. Let $K=k(V)$ and choose a pulled-back basis of the ambient generic vector space. In a Grassmannian chart, represent this subspace by a matrix $(I\mid M)$. Horizontality says that every entry of $M$ has zero differential. Since $K$ is separably generated over the perfect field $k$, the kernel of $d\colon K\to\Omega_{K/k}^1$ is $K^p$. Taking $p$-th roots of the entries of $M$ therefore defines a subspace before Frobenius pullback.

Saturate this subspace to a subsheaf $B_0\subset E$. At every codimension-one point the relevant torsion-free quotients are free over the discrete valuation ring. Frobenius is flat on the smooth variety, so $F_V^*B_0$ is saturated there and agrees with $B$ there: they have the same generic subspace. Consequently $$\deg(B)=p\deg(B_0),\qquad
  \mu(B_0)=\frac{\mu(B)}p>
  \frac{\mu(F_V^*E)}p=\mu(E),$$ contradicting semistability of $E$. ◻

</details>

The general connection criterion is also stated in (Langer 2004b, Corollary 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-41"></a>[[langer2004](#ref-langer2004)]. The argument above makes explicit why it applies to the present nef slope.

<a id="stmt-lem-destabilizing-foliation"></a>
<div id="lem:destabilizing-foliation" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (The first destabilizing term is a foliation). *If $T_V$ is unstable, its first Harder–Narasimhan term $A$ is closed under Lie brackets and under $p$-th powers of derivations.*

</div>

This rank-one and rank-two argument follows the foliation criterion in (C. Xu and Zhang 2019, Lemma 2.9) <a id="pm-cite-42"></a>[[xuzhang2019](#ref-xuzhang2019)]; we give the slope calculation for the nef product used here.

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-destabilizing-foliation"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-destabilizing-foliation</summary>

*Proof.* Put $Q=T_V/A$ and $a=\mu(A)$. By Equation <a href="#eq:slope-numerical-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:slope-numerical-input">[eq:slope-numerical-input]</a>, $\mu(T_V)=0$, so $$a>0,\qquad \mu_{\max}(Q)<a.$$ The rank of $A$ is one or two. Work where $A$ and $Q$ are bundles. The bracket modulo $A$ is an $\mathcal O_V$-linear map $\bigwedge^2A\to Q$. It vanishes automatically in rank one. In rank two, $Q$ has rank one and slope $-2a$, whereas $\bigwedge^2A$ has slope $2a$; Equation <a href="#eq:slope-hom-vanishing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:slope-hom-vanishing">[eq:slope-hom-vanishing]</a> again makes the map zero.

<a id="eq:p-curvature-obstruction"></a>
Once bracket closure holds, the $p$-th power of derivations modulo $A$ defines an $\mathcal O_V$-linear map $$F_V^*A\longrightarrow Q.
  \label{eq:p-curvature-obstruction}$$ For completeness, the two restricted-Lie identities needed for this assertion can be seen directly. For local derivations $u,v\in A$, set $w=tu+v$ with $t$ a formal scalar. In characteristic $p$, $$\frac{d}{dt}w^p
  =\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}w^juw^{p-1-j}
  =\operatorname{ad}(w)^{p-1}(u).$$ The last equality follows from $\binom{p-1}{j}\equiv(-1)^j\pmod p$. Every coefficient of the right-hand side lies in the bracket-closed module $A$. The mixed coefficients of $w^p-t^pu^p-v^p$, whose degrees in $t$ lie between $1$ and $p-1$, thus belong to $A$. This proves additivity modulo $A$.

For a local function $f$, expanding $(fu)^p$ as a differential operator gives $$(fu)^p-f^pu^p=\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}c_ju^j.$$ The left side is a derivation. At the generic point, if $u\ne0$, choose $x$ with $u(x)\ne0$. For a largest index $j\ge2$ with $c_j\ne0$, the $j$-fold commutator with multiplication by $x$ is $j!c_j(u(x))^j\ne0$, while it must vanish for a derivation. Hence only the $j=1$ term can remain. The scalar discrepancy is therefore a multiple of $u$, proving Frobenius linearity modulo $A$ and hence Equation <a href="#eq:p-curvature-obstruction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:p-curvature-obstruction">[eq:p-curvature-obstruction]</a>.

If $\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}A=1$, its source has slope $pa>a>\mu_{\max}(Q)$ and the map is zero. Suppose $\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}A=2$. In this case $$\mu(Q)=-2a,\qquad \mu_{\max}(\Omega_V^1)=2a.$$ If $F_V^*A$ is semistable, its minimum slope is $pa>-2a$. Otherwise let $L$ be its first Harder–Narasimhan term and let $R=F_V^*A/L$; both have rank one. Lemma <a href="#lem:connection-destabilization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:connection-destabilization">6</a> supplies a nonzero map $$L\longrightarrow R\otimes\Omega_V^1.$$ Since $R$ has rank one, the slope rules give $$\mu(L)-\mu(R)\le2a,\qquad
  \mu(L)+\mu(R)=2pa.$$ It follows that $$\mu_{\min}(F_V^*A)=\mu(R)\ge pa-a>-2a.$$ Thus Equation <a href="#eq:slope-hom-vanishing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:slope-hom-vanishing">[eq:slope-hom-vanishing]</a> makes Equation <a href="#eq:p-curvature-obstruction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:p-curvature-obstruction">[eq:p-curvature-obstruction]</a> zero in either case. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-18"></a>
#### The inseparable quotient and its canonical divisor

We spell out the quotient construction to keep track of the sign in the canonical formula. These constructions and their use for tangent semistability also appear in (C. Xu and Zhang 2019, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-43"></a>[[xuzhang2019](#ref-xuzhang2019)].

<a id="stmt-lem-foliation-quotient"></a>
<div id="lem:foliation-quotient" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:foliation-canonical"></a>
**Lemma 8** (Quotient by the distribution). *Let $A\subset T_V$ be a saturated bracket-closed and $p$-closed subsheaf of rank $d$. There is a finite purely inseparable map $\pi\colon V\to W$ to a normal projective variety, with a factorization $$V\xrightarrow{\pi}W\xrightarrow{\rho}V^{(1)}$$ of relative Frobenius. On open sets whose complements have codimension at least two, the canonical bundles satisfy $$\omega_V\simeq\pi^*\omega_W\otimes(\det A)^{\otimes(p-1)}.
  \label{eq:foliation-canonical}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-foliation-quotient"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-foliation-quotient</summary>

*Proof.* On an affine open with ring $R$, let $$P=\{f\in R: u(f)=0\text{ for every local section }u\in A\}.$$ The ring $P$ lies between $R^p$ and $R$. Since $R$ is finite over $R^p$, $P$ is finite over $R^p$ and $R$ is finite over $P$. These invariant rings glue to the asserted factorization of Frobenius; in particular both maps are finite and their underlying maps of topological spaces are homeomorphisms. The ring $P$ is normal. Indeed, an element of $\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}(P)$ integral over $P$ is integral over $R$, hence belongs to the normal ring $R$, and all the derivations annihilate it. Thus it belongs to $P$. The variety $W$ is projective because it is finite over the projective variety $V^{(1)}$.

Remove the codimension-at-least-two loci where $A$ or $T_V/A$ fails to be locally free. Near any remaining point choose functions $x_1,\ldots,x_d$ whose differentials restrict to a basis of $A^\vee$, and choose the dual local derivations $u_1,\ldots,u_d$. Thus $u_i(x_j)=\delta_{ij}$. Bracket closure implies $[u_i,u_j]\in A$; this bracket annihilates every $x_l$ and is therefore zero. Likewise $u_i^p\in A$ annihilates all the $x_l$, so $u_i^p=0$.

<a id="eq:foliation-local-presentation"></a>
These commuting nilpotent derivations give an explicit invariant-ring presentation. For one derivation $u$ with $u(x)=1$, the coefficient $(p-1)!^{-1}u^{p-1}(f)$ lies in its kernel. Subtract its product with $x^{p-1}$ and repeat with successively smaller powers. This writes every $f$ uniquely as a polynomial of degree less than $p$ in $x$, with coefficients in the kernel of $u$. Applying this procedure successively to the commuting $u_i$ gives $$R=\bigoplus_{0\le I_1,\ldots,I_d<p}P\,x^I
  \simeq
  P[X_1,\ldots,X_d]/(X_1^p-c_1,\ldots,X_d^p-c_d),
  \qquad c_i=x_i^p\in P.
  \label{eq:foliation-local-presentation}$$ Uniqueness follows by applying the same successive highest derivatives to a putative relation. In particular, $\pi$ is locally free of degree $p^d$ on this open set.

<a id="eq:foliation-cotangent"></a>
We may further remove the singular locus of $W$ and its inverse image; normality and finiteness ensure that these removed sets also have codimension at least two. The presentation in Equation <a href="#eq:foliation-local-presentation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:foliation-local-presentation">[eq:foliation-local-presentation]</a> now yields the exact sequence of vector bundles $$0\longrightarrow F_V^*(A^\vee)
  \longrightarrow\pi^*\Omega_W^1
